## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

**Flip 1 — certify authorization, meter resources** (LLP 2000.000 §6). The
certificate keeps every read site's shape, uniformity, completeness class
and prediction eligibility, and stops pricing bytes and steps. A
deterministic meter charges examined rows, index entries, rule probes,
steps, heap, output and rows written as the program runs, on the server and
in the client interpreter alike. A query cut by the meter returns `capped`
with no continuation past the cut, the same observable outcome as a static
ceiling cut today; a mutation aborts with `E_BOUND` naming actual work; a
job is chunked. `check --cost` prints the structural cost class; `test`
prints measured meters on the hub fixture.

**Flip 2 — the viewer's partition is the unit of sync** (LLP 2000.000 §7).
`sync to <audience> last N by <index>` on a table makes the engine maintain,
per principal, the rows that principal may read, as a change log with a
horizon; a table without the word is online-only, and `check` says which
rule term prevented inversion. The client holds a replica of its partitions,
one watermark each, a durable outbox with client-minted ids, and the
interpreter; queries run locally and are `complete` inside the horizon.
Revocation is a scope tombstone; `until-revoked` and `delivered-history`
survive as the client's leave policy. Two client tiers — online-only
without a store, local-first with one — share one language and one server.

**The vocabulary is fixed before the mechanism** (LLP 2000.000 §8):

```ts
type Read<T> = { data: T; complete: boolean; fresh: boolean; since?: number; next?: Cursor }
            | { loading: true } | { denied: Refusal };
row.pending: boolean;
type Write = { state: "pending" } | { state: "sent"; seq: number } | { state: "failed"; why: Refusal };
type Link = "online" | "offline" | "signed-out";
```

A screen that needs a word not on that card is a defect in the mechanism,
not a gap in the card.

**The hub comes first** (LLP 2000.000 §11): the Clique generator's `u0001`
with 1,000 memberships, `u0500` with 500 follows, and about 100,000
messages are the first fixture; day-two changes are designed before the
day-one screen; the native client is in slice one; physical floors are
measured before targets; the simulator is the primary harness.

## 3. The bakeoff protocol

Shared with Snapback 3; LLP 2000 or a sibling record should adopt the same
text so that the two tracks are measured by one instrument.

**Brief.** [`benchmarks/briefs/social-messenger.md`](../benchmarks/briefs/social-messenger.md)
(Clique) M1–M8, plus
[`benchmarks/briefs/messaging-day2-evolution.md`](../benchmarks/briefs/messaging-day2-evolution.md).
The field-work application (LLP 2000 §4) tests generality after the first
slice, on whichever tracks survive the clock.

**Fixture.** The Clique generator at the instrument freeze LLP 1023 and
LLP 1024 record (`f40559f37722c79178be664a11f8a6485f1508fd`): hub `u0001`
with 100 groups and 900 DMs, `u0500` with 500 follows, and the 100,015
messages of the retained 0.0.25 replay database. The same fixture for every
track and for the control.

**Harness and judge.** [`benchmarks/protocol.md`](../benchmarks/protocol.md);
the same three scorers as the 2026-09-05 run (gpt-6-astra, grok-4.6,
muse-spark); Convex as the control arm; published packages; fresh cold
agents with only the installed package and the task. Neither lead runs the
comparison or grades their own track. The author rules on the numbers.

**Decision rule, pre-registered.**
# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.
# LLP 2000.000: Snapback 3 — keep the core, flip two substrates, start at hub scale

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Draft
**Role:** Second opinion on LLP 2000; since 2026-09-12 also the design brief Snapback 4 builds (LLP 3000)
**Systems:** Snapback 3 product architecture, bounded computation, client data model, client API vocabulary, language and tooling, effects runtime, verification (simulation), performance budgets, process
**Author:** Claude Fable 5.1, at Charlie Cheever's request
**Date:** 2026-09-12
**Revised:** 2026-09-12 (adopted as the Snapback 4 design brief by LLP 3000 decision 3); 2026-09-12 (initial)
**Related:** [LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md) (parent), [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the Snapback 4 root that builds this brief), LLP 0000, [LLP 0329](./0329-agent-native-rebuild-diagnosis.research.md), [LLP 0329.014.004](./0329.014.004-bounded-data-programs-v1-design.rfc.md), [LLP 0329.016](./0329.016-agentic-dev-loop-from-scratch.rfc.md), [LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md), [LLP 1001](./1001-query-engine.spec.md), [LLP 1002](./1002-dev-loop.spec.md), [LLP 1012](./1012-local-first-v1.spec.md), [LLP 1018.003](./1018.003-truthful-and-composed-client.decision.md), [LLP 1018.008](./1018.008-elegance-after-the-corrections.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.007](./1018.008.007-affordable-offline-acquisition.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.008](./1018.008.008-locality-of-application-changes.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.009](./1018.008.009-residual-ergonomic-gaps.research.md), [LLP 1019](./1019-derived-delivery-custody.rfc.md), [LLP 1020](./1020-day-two-evolution-and-legible-ceilings.rfc.md), [LLP 1023](./1023-clique-builder-jump.rfc.md), [LLP 1024](./1024-single-lane-delivered-search.rfc.md), [LLP 1025](./1025-digest-final-emission-at-hub-scale.research.md), `rules/RULES.md`, `rules/NOT-DOING.md`, `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-05-clique-full-3x3/holistic-scores.md`

## 1. Summary

LLP 2000 is right that the whole application is the unit of design, right
that ownership comes before abstraction, and right to ask for deterministic
simulation and an early real deployment. Its weakness is that it treats the
two structural choices that produced Snapback 2's costs as open research
questions, and then proposes a first experiment at exactly the scale where
those costs are invisible. This document takes a position on both, names
what to keep from Snapback 2 verbatim, and rewrites the first slice so it
can fail for the right reasons.

The position, in one paragraph. Snapback 2's *core* is excellent and should
be copied: the rule language and its uniformity analysis, the structural-fact
family, one data language, typed states with no silence, refusals that paste
a rewrite, personas, the successor classifier, the derived per-recipient
log, the loop verbs and their sub-second budgets. Two *substrates* under
that core are where the record shows the hours went, and both should be
flipped rather than re-investigated: (1) the static certificate should keep
certifying **authorization and shape** and stop pricing **bytes and steps**,
which move to a deterministic runtime meter with honest `capped`/abort
outcomes; and (2) the client should hold **a replica of the viewer's declared
partition** — the local-first shape every shipped sync engine and Snapback
2's own `deliver` log converge on — instead of a per-query result cache
proven by receipts. Those two flips dissolve most of the machinery that
leaked into the application (receipts, coverage, halos, validators, grants,
ID slices, drains, `withHistory`, follower inboxes, per-table retention
words) and turn the 28-second acquisition into a bulk stream whose floor is
the device's write speed. The first slice should be built on the **hub
fixture** (1,000 memberships, 500 follows, 100,000 messages), with the
day-two change designed before the day-one screen, a native client in the
first slice, and a deterministic simulator as the primary test harness.

Three claims this document makes that LLP 2000 does not:

1. **The costs have two named causes, and both are decisions, not
   mysteries.** Byte-priced static certification and the receipt-proven
   result cache account for every ergonomic residual in LLP 1018.008.009
   except documentation. §2 shows the mapping.
2. **The first experiment must include the hub.** Snapback 2 was "elegant on
   day one and hostile on day two" (LLP 1020) and broke at 1,000 memberships
   (LLPs 1023, 1024, 1025). A two-principal slice will pass and teach nothing.
3. **The process is part of the product.** Seventy-one LLPs, over two
   thousand commits, and seven ceiling raises in fourteen days produced a
   sound system nobody can hold in their head. Snapback 3 needs one owner on
   the core, a simulator instead of a test matrix, and budgets anchored to
   competitors so that raising one means admitting a loss.

## 2. What the record says, read as causes

The evidence LLP 2000 §2 cites is right; its reading is too gentle. Every
row below is a Snapback 2 cost recorded in the corpus, with the substrate
that produced it. Numbers are the corpus's own and retain its scope limits.

| Recorded cost | Where | Cause |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Adding `editedAt: time ?` (44 bytes × 244 retained copies = 10,736) pushed `messages.searchAll` to 4,199,688 closure bytes against 4,194,304; the builder shrank a page from 20 to 19 | LLP 1018.008.008; 0.0.21 day-two diary | **Static byte pricing.** Closure bytes are a bound on what a receipt could ship; the product paid for the bound, not the work |
| The 1,000-membership digest fold certifies at 9,922,698 heap and 8,691,704 closure bytes and is refused; the recipient-keyed form is admitted at 2,733,253 / 1,848,418 | LLP 1025 §3 | **Static byte pricing** (heap = steps × 32 + closure scratch). A runtime meter would have run the fold and reported actual bytes |
| A message page is 50, not 200, because a row "prices at 16,016 bytes" and 200 exceed the 1 MiB output ceiling | LLP 1012 §3 | **Static byte pricing** of the worst-case row width |
| A per-recipient search projection is refused at 20,863,641 reserved steps against 1,000,000 | LLP 1024 §1 | **Static step pricing** of a derivation the engine would perform incrementally |
| Full acquisition of 10,008 messages: 67.4 s → 53.6 s → 32.6 s → 28.4 s against a 2–3 s objective; 201 requests, 200 acknowledgement mutations, 612 IndexedDB transactions; PowerSync reports 10,000 rows in 0.36 s native and 2.5 s on web | LLP 1018.008.007; LLP 1018.008.009 §4 | **Result cache, not replica.** Every 50-row page is a certified query, a receipt, a durable acknowledgement mutation, and a store swap |
| Adding a count to a thread makes the composite `partial` offline whenever count authority is missing, so the template declares an independent `history` query and `withHistory` bindings; 27 files / 1,718 lines → 28 / 1,751 after the fix | LLP 1018.008 F2; LLP 1018.008.009 §2 | **Result cache.** The count is not a fact the receipt covers, so the client cannot compose locally |
| `shared/thread.ts` describes table identity, row selection, order, create membership, draft fields and mutation targets a second time | LLP 1018.008 F2 | **Result cache.** Items must be reconstructed from answers because there is no local table to read |
| Sending stalled with 85 IDs remaining against a certified 352-ID slice on an 8,192-ID grant; recovery needed a "Sync now" action | LLP 1018.008 F1 | **Result cache + engine-minted IDs.** Prediction needs server ids before commit, so ids are granted in slices |
| A follower browser tab writes through a durable inbox row; the SDK carries `forwarding`, `writer-unresponsive`, `inbox-full`, `follower` states | LLP 1018.004 | **Result cache.** One writer per partition image; other tabs cannot mutate a receipt-proven image |
| Global search over 1,000 memberships costs ~1,010 HTTP requests through a client N-way merge | LLP 1024 §2 | **Result cache** (no local index over the partition) + **static pricing** (the server-side projection is refused) |
| The complete local runtime grew 7.8 KB → 89,843 gzip bytes; the ceiling was raised 34,000 → 50,000 → 66,000 → 82,000 → 82,500 → 84,000 → 85,000 → 90,000 | LLP 1000 §"Revised"; `rules/NOT-DOING.md` §Moves; LLP 1018.008 §10 | **Result cache.** The inputs list of `metrics/twin.jsonl` is coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, posting trees, fact integrity, swap journals — receipt machinery, not the interpreter |
| `Snapshot` carries `data, next, partial, receipt{state, source, seq, next, reason, rule, sites}, freshness{live, staleSince, connection}, local{predicted, retained, caughtUp, watermark}, refusal, status, reason` | LLP 1018.003 §1 | Both. Every field is a true fact about the mechanism; almost none is a fact about the application |
| `Schema::rule_hash` binds every queued write to the complete schema; an optional column on the subject table invalidates unrelated evidence | LLP 1018.002; LLP 1018.008.008 | **Result cache.** Evidence identity exists because held answers must be re-authorized; a replica re-runs the mutation at the server and needs no evidence identity |

Against that, what scored: on the 2026-09-05 three-scorer Clique run
Snapback 2 beat Convex on authorization (82 vs 79) and offline (81 vs 44)
and lost on queries and live (60 vs 85), client integration (59 vs 79),
setup and docs (55 vs 79), diagnostics and loop (66 vs 77), operations (53
vs 76) and scale (62 vs 80); overall 66 vs 81. The later single-lane 0.0.21
and 0.0.25 scores of 82 and 85 are real improvement and not a three-scorer
comparison. The wins are the core; the losses are composition, discovery,
scale and operations — the substrates and what sits on them.

# LLP 3000.036.004: Security invariants and exposure

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Accepted (SEC design revisions 3/5 and SEC2 amendment/implementation round 5: Muse and fresh Codex READY; qualification in progress)
**Systems:** Snapback 4 Quarry authorization, check, audit, generated security tests, validation
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever (SEC)
**Date:** 2026-10-18
**Revised:** 2026-10-24 (DX9 wave-26 amendments; Muse and fresh Codex READY); 2026-10-24 (Integration 24 §15.1 boundary clarification, governed by LLP 3000.036.004.000 §9); 2026-10-23 (SEC7 §16 design: Muse and fresh Codex READY; implementation qualification in progress); previously 2026-10-21 (SEC5 §14 and SEC6 §15; both independently reviewed; SEC6 scoped qualification complete; code snapshot4578e98bed)
**Related:** [LLP 3000.036](./3000.036-what-the-app-farm-asks-of-the-rule-language.rfc.md), [LLP 3000.035](./3000.035-quarry-semantics-in-lean.plan.md), [LLP 3000.014](./3000.014-a-child-reads-through-its-parent.rfc.md), [LLP 3000.038](./3000.038-file-assets.rfc.md)

## 19. Wave-28 output and witness decisions (DX11)

Commissioned by Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-25. This amendment supersedes
§18's default questionnaire and exact-set family command contract. It does
not change SEC10's detector or gate semantics.

A passing ordinary `check` prints one verdict line and the existing bounded
language-enforcement summary. Only gates and reachable authored-invariant
refutations block. Advisory decisions and compiler notes require `check
--review` (also `--verbose` or JSON); the verdict counts them and names that
command. A pending gate always prints its individual command. JSON keeps all
sites, IDs, bindings, acknowledgments and evidence, regardless of display mode.

Decision identity excludes incidental locations and wall-clock campaign
results. A single canonical note set feeds JSON, display and acknowledgment.
Repeated `--ack-security ID` and comma-separated IDs with one `--reason` are
validated completely and committed atomically under the existing lock/source
race checks. An invalid member writes nothing. An audience family command
expands only to current advisory members; prior member records survive member
additions. Each new or changed member remains pending. Previously displayed
family aliases may acknowledge only their still-current original members;
never silently acknowledge a newly added member. The generated family manifest
retains each displayed family id and its individual id/binding pairs. Historical
aliases require every original member still to match; changed/deleted members
refuse the whole batch. New members are not selected. The verdict counts still
pending advisories and points to --review; their individual commands remain
there. No new member changes a previously recorded member's acknowledgment.
Batch expansion uses one captured sources/analyzer/note set and final source
revalidation under the writer lock. One invalid ID writes no entries. Unknown IDs explain that
they are stale, from a different source selection, or not decision IDs and
point to `check --review --json`. Relevant semantic dependency changes still
invalidate individual acknowledgments; unrelated declarations and comments do
not. Version-1 records remain readable; witness-bearing decisions require a
new global analyzer revision `sec11-1`, staling all older acknowledgments.
The witness-associated codes in this increment are N_APPROVAL_NOT_ENFORCED,
N_ARGUMENT_GRANT, N_INSERT_WRITE_INTENT and N_DERIVED_WRITE_AUTHORITY. Other
questions explicitly retain their unsupported/no-witness status. The candidate
is bound by its code, normalized diagnostic and semantic source dependency
slice; operation/caller/arguments/images, generated ids, sample order and
campaign timing are display/storage evidence only, never ID or binding inputs.

A concrete campaign result associated with a question is shown as a witness:
operation, caller, arguments, admitted effect, old/new image and candidate
invariant. Questions may propose additional candidate invariants to exercise
this output path: matching grant-column names to role literals, matching
approval prefixes to boolean triggers, and reusing the edit restriction already
printed by the write-advice detector. They are labeled inferred hypotheses and
never alter the gate registry, authored invariants or check exit status.
A decreasing upsert example replays two admitted writes on the engine from
the observed generated fixture; it never fabricates old/new values.
It remains evidence about an inferred specification question,
never an authored contradiction or proof. Unsupported or exhausted campaigns
say no witness was obtained, without inventing behavior. Acknowledgment
records retain the displayed witness and candidate, alongside the rationale
and semantic binding. Upsert wording explicitly says that its existing-row
arm **edits**. Derived evidence cannot be used to override a reachable authored
invariant refutation. The new witness path uses generated fixtures only, never application credential
fixtures. Schema-typed text, bytes, JSON, asset values and unknown compound
values are replaced by <redacted>; argument strings are redacted even when an
enum. Retained scalar numerics/booleans, generated principals and row ids,
source enum state names, operation names and candidate predicates describe
the behavior. History records contain sanitized arguments and state only.
A sentinel regression checks opaque redaction. Witness caches are generated
presentation evidence and never decide authority or a verdict.

The adjoining developer-loop changes preserve the contracts of LLP
3000.000.000.001 and LLP 3000.015: refusal attribution and read-filter evidence
are developer-owned diagnostics, never new disclosure in public refusal
responses. Hooks expose `usePage.loading` and `usePage.why` as projections of
`page.read`, retaining its completeness/freshness evidence. A first-run check
hint makes the pinned React starter discoverable for projects that skipped
init, without modifying their files. Staging reports name persisted clock
offset; upload overload guidance names serialization/retry; proof docs say
text must be on screen, and verification recipes use `serve-check`. Expo's
starter offers a web file picker and native image picker.

Qualification: targeted CLI/client regressions, witnesses for wave-28 011 F1,
011 F2 and 019 F1, copies of all fifty schemas with baseline and SEC10 codes,
packed consumer snippets/starters, affected selector and reference check.
Darwin is unavailable in this Linux lane and is explicitly skipped. Finite
campaign replay is not a security proof or a promise of builder preference.
The CLI tests cover all JSON decision IDs, atomic mixed-invalid batches,
comment/independent-edit stability, historical family additions, real grant
attribution without public disclosure and authored-refutation precedence.
React lifecycle tests exercise loading, denied and stale/partial page evidence;
upload retry tests retain retry bounds and assert guidance. Packed tests run
staging/probe scripts and typecheck/export Expo's picker. Clock tests check the
printed retained offset. The 450-project new-gate census is in SEC10's lane:
DX11 introduces no gate. --review retains the compact nondecision notes and
individual questions (including the complete audience-family site list);
JSON retains original diagnostics, never an invented questionnaire ID.
Two independent reviews (Muse and fresh Codex) are retained under llp/reviews.

## 18. Wave-26 security decisions (DX9)

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-24. Revision 3: Muse and fresh Codex READY; implementation is in the DX9 commits. Qualification and limitations: WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md at the worktree root.
This supersedes §17.3's prohibition on family disposition only for the
advisory audience/restatement family. Gates remain individual specification
decisions, including every new SEC10 gate; one gate registry decides ranking.
The default check summary names the number of pending decisions and the next
command, even when callers read only ^check:. Each review-tier item appears
once as a question with one-line alternatives: repair enforcement, record an
intentional specification decision, or add independent intent/test evidence.
Performance/design information remains separate. JSON retains every original
diagnostic, site, ID, source binding and acknowledgment. Gate explanation and
disposition command share one decision entry, never a second note listing.

check --ack-security audience:<family-id> --reason '<spec rationale>' records
one rationale for the exact displayed set of current non-gated audience notes.
The eligible codes are exactly N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE, N_ROLE_WIDE_READ,
N_ROLE_WIDE_WRITE and N_BROAD_READ, excluding any code in GATED_NOTE_CODES.
The family ID hashes analyzer revision sec9-3, their sorted individual IDs and
semantic source bindings;
each member is still written as an individual existing version-1 record. A
new member, changed dependency, unknown/stale family, blank reason, compilation
failure or reachable refutation cannot be cleared. No gate, derived contract,
non-audience heuristic, or authored contradiction enters this family. Existing
writer lock and source race checks bind the complete set. All member entries
are added in memory and published in one temp+rename of security-reviews.json;
there is no partial-family write. A crash before rename leaves the prior file.
The individual record analyzer revision also becomes sec9-3.
Output names the member subjects and count so the command is reviewable.

The static rule analyzer's Verdict.direct_rule=Proved alongside
Verdict.status=Unproved is rendered as
"enforced by rule; additional paths not proved", with exact blockers in verbose
and JSON (Verdict.reasons, Verdict.obligations and their paths/status/repair).
Whole-invariant Proved/Refuted/Unproved semantics never change, and
no finite campaign promotes a proof. A check line counts asset-bearing columns (ColumnType.contains_asset),
Table.column_reads entries, and N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION diagnostics. Asset owner
custody is the runtime asset ledger/placement contract, not a linter theorem.
Role diagnostics use the existing lexical authority walker (helper depth 32)
and existing bounded privilege analyzer; the line says "detected (bounded
analysis)" and does not characterize unreachable/unsupported paths as safe. Never print an unconditional zero-role-hole claim.

The old N_SWEEP_ONLY_DEADLINE detector is folded into the single
N_SWEEP_DEADLINE path detector (its existing why explanation remains readable).
It correlates a scheduled status assignment and a mutation
guard by the same table, column and literal state/polarity. Sharing a status
column is insufficient. Mutations admitting the state the sweep removes need
the deadline question; mutations requiring a disjoint state are quiet. Unknown
state relationships stay explicit conservative questions. For each actual write, the lexical authority walker supplies its prefix require
statements and the chosen branch condition or its negation. Finite bool/enum
states are evaluated under those guards with unknown treated as possibly
admitted. Empty intersection of the job's and mutation's admitted pre-states
suppresses the question; otherwise it is conservative. Only recognized guards on the same table/deadline and resolved read-key identity that exclude the
sweep's due-time window suppress it. Normalize direct field-versus-now
comparisons into before/equal/after masks; transpose reversed comparisons,
complement not, intersect and, and union or only when both arms are known.
Unknown/affine-offset relationships retain the question. A <= sweep is excluded
by >, not >= (which overlaps equality) or <=. PeriodEnd in the job denotes its
elapsed boundary; mutation guards compare against now. Tests cover polarity,
equality overlap, negation and sibling branches.
Alias names are resolved to the underlying read/table/key; string alias-name
equality alone is neither required nor sufficient for suppression. Job clocks
cannot suppress mutation advice. A server-now guard must dominate the particular guarded write path, not occur in an unrelated arm.
Tests include unrelated deadlines on shared status columns and actual races.

## 1. Contract

The wave-16 bakeoff found rules enforced correctly but policies encoded wrongly.
A declaration of intent is independent of the rules implementing it. `check`
reports each declaration as **proved**, **refuted**, or **unproved**. Refuted
means a concrete, replayed database/rule counterexample, never merely a Boolean
assignment. Unproved names the unsupported path or incomplete search. Neither
unproved nor a finite passing test campaign may be described as proved.

Implementer: SEC in this worktree, starting 2026-10-18. The author commissioned
this semantic extension and two independent design reviews. No version bump
or publication is part of this lane. STAGE owns provisioning/password dev doors;
RULES5 owns live-parent rule semantics; FRONT owns general diagnostic triage.

## 2. Surface

Use the existing rule vocabulary rather than invent a second policy language:

```
invariant notes.read only .patient = viewer or .clinician = viewer
invariant requests.update only .requester != viewer
invariant members.role write only exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']
invariant photos always .image != null
```

The first target is a table, the second component an effect (`read`, `insert`,
`update`, `delete`) or a column followed by `write`. `write` checks insert and
update, including both images for ordinary updates; delete authority has its
own declaration. A column invariant only concerns writes to that column, but
must include every effective admission path. Owner-only field patches bypass
an ordinary table update rule; invariants affected by `written by` stay
unproved until that bypass is modeled. An implication from a denied table
rule must never prove such an owned-field claim.
`always` is a row-integrity assertion. Predicates parse as ordinary Quarry
expressions; unsupported policy expressions are reported unproved with the
reason. Unknown tables/columns/effects are authoring errors. Multiple source
files and multiple declarations are supported. For update intent `.field` means both old and next images for mutable
columns; named transitions require an explicit reviewed reduction or stay
unproved. Once-judged immutable/id rules need a proved equal-image reduction
or stay unproved. Both upsert arms and `insert if absent` are included, with
their own collision/refusal observations. `always` quantifies over every
committed row image from insert/update/upsert and trusted seed/import/restore/
activation, not merely mutation inserts. Constraint-based row proof must use
the actual validation on every ingress, including defaults and the exact
non-whitespace minimum function, or stay unproved.

No declaration changes runtime
authority or replaces a rule. No implicit service-seat exemption is added.

The proof fragment uses bound rule predicates. A row assertion can be proved
from its required column type/constraints (not an optional type), or otherwise
remains unproved. Aggregate/transition/temporal assertions outside that fragment
remain explicitly unproved, not syntactically discarded. Syntax with `now`,
counts, or other program expressions must retain that reason. This is a safe
extension point, not a claim of a complete solver for arbitrary Quarry.

## 3. Sound proof boundary

For a table effect, prove `admission => policy` for **every database snapshot**,
row image, ordinary authenticated viewer (including service seats) and clock.
The engine system principal is explicitly outside this quantification: it is
the trusted seed/import/job boundary, never an ordinary user or an implicit
service-seat exemption. Tables touched by system/native/job/worker paths remain
unproved absent a reviewed reduction, and audit lists those writers. This overapproximates every reachable history:
an old assignment and a changed current role are independent facts. Grant
creation never proves a current role. Required role predicates must be true at
the access boundary. No fixture-specific assumption enters a proof.

Translate Boolean connectives compositionally, retaining true/false/unknown
truth for atomic predicates. Unknown never admits and `not unknown` is unknown.
Atomic facts are keyed by their full bound structure, including table, index,
keys, named equalities and old/next image. Symmetric equality may canonicalize by operand reordering only, preserving
Value variants (Id is not Text), table, index, keys, image and predicate kind;
other equalities and probes stay independent. A finite exhaustive implication
check over this abstraction is sound because it includes every concrete
valuation, even impossible ones. Cap atoms/work; cap exhaustion is unproved.
Do not add database consistency axioms without a separate soundness argument.
## 17. SEC8: actionable evidence, honest campaigns and durable decisions

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-22. Status: Implemented. This
wave-24 amendment supersedes the name-list restriction in §16.9 and whole-source
review identity in §§16.2/16.5. DX7 owns upgrade/gate messaging and clock/proof UI.

### 17.1 Presence and lifecycle questions

Optional media stays advisory without independent intent. Both the note and the
specification questionnaire offer `invariant TABLE always .COLUMN != null` only
for permanent integrity, and `invariant TABLE.insert only .COLUMN != null` for
creation-only presence (later redaction remains possible). The upload gate also
recognizes positive non-null conjuncts in independent insert/write intent for
that exact column; OR and unknown predicates cannot establish this gate.
The upload recipe begins with that decision. Never compile the questionnaire or
infer mandatory evidence from natural language. Existing explicit-intent gates
remain the only gates. A parent with an independently attached media child gets
an advisory when a literal enum transition lacks a dominating child existence
requirement; text photo/URL fields are explicitly labelled name-based questions.

Stale eligibility considers any literal enum update distinct from the creation
state, without interpreting words such as filled/confirmed. It still requires
mutable eligibility on the same resource and keeps the exact recheck precision
of §16.9. Approval questions cover approval-named flags ignored by lifecycle
writes, and fields checked by an authorized decision but editable without that
decision's lifecycle restrictions. Evidence replacement after approval is included.
Time questions cover overdue/missed states without a server-clock check,
deadlines bounded against now where a dependent stored deadline exists, and
365-day multiples used as anniversaries. A guard-state question asks whether a
boolean requirement should be consumed by a transition. All these are bounded
structural/name-based questions, never proof, inferred intent or new gates.

### 17.2 Campaign honesty and useful generated roots

Every invariant outside the finite evaluator's fragment is named by target and
source site in default test output with its reason; a separate evaluation-gap
count prevents zero violations being read as full invariant coverage. Static
unproved remains unproved. No unknown evaluation becomes policy false. Inherited
raw read-through child assertions remain explicit evaluation gaps; this amendment
does not add raw evaluator capability. Future support requires §8.1 seed ingress,
committed readback, parent lifetime/retirement and final-exists validation. A
snapshot-only comparison never sets refuted.
Generated required uploads use real verified bytes and actor-owned fresh ledger
entries, satisfying declared byte limits; no argument/type/admission bypass.
Generated creators reserve at most two rows per target table. After an admitted
creator tuple, try a tuple differing in at least one boolean before another actor
can consume the second row slot; this is not complete Cartesian coverage. Keep
the existing 16 tuple/2048 creator-call initialization limits and report omitted
boolean combinations. Directed exploration uses depth 12, 4096 aggregate attempts,
eight argument tuples per operation/world, three distinct successors per operation,
and a five-world beam (at most 16 retained worlds including current/next/successors).
These bounds are shared, not multiplied per creator boolean. Breadth remains 512.
Check retains its shared 512-work/1500ms cap; no new cap confers proof. Report every
cut and retain generated provenance.

### 17.3 Presentation and review identity

Human output collapses restatement and role-wide questions into one questionnaire
per table. Full original diagnostics, audiences and source sites remain available
in JSON and verbose audit. The generated draft remains the complete questionnaire.
Advisories accept the same per-note rationale/disposition command as gates and
print a discoverable route to it; no bulk acknowledgment and no proof promotion.

A review ID binds analyzer revision, diagnostic code, subject and stable semantic
content, excluding line/column locations. Its source binding is a semantic slice:
the relevant rule/column and its dependency closure, or conservatively the source
declaration and referenced/writing declarations for complex flow notes. Comments,
line shifts and independent declarations do not invalidate it. Changing a relevant
rule/column, helper, grant writer, role mutator or independent intent does.
Conservative fallback scope must be explicit and tested; never silently use an
empty dependency set. Existing version-1 records remain readable but become stale
once at analyzer revision change. Keep source-race checks, lock, atomic rename,
invalid-file refusal and no-writing-on-refutation semantics unchanged. Dispositions
remain source-control review records, never application authority.

### 17.4 Qualification

Positive and suppression regressions cover each detector and generated upload
roots, evaluation gaps, boolean alternatives, durable advisory/gate dispositions
and precise invalidation. Re-measure copies of all thirteen non-judgment wave-24
findings in three modes (no intent, authored intent, one independent spec line),
separating advice, gates and reachable refutations. Report corpus gate counts and
inspect every new gate across 450 projects. Run the commissioned full verification
and packed-guide checks; do not claim an unavailable check passed.

### 17.5 Review resolutions and exact finite fragments

Each original advisory remains exit-0/display-only and independently addressable
by its JSON securityReviews ID. A collapsed N_TABLE_QUESTIONNAIRE has no review ID;
it is only a human projection. Verbose/JSON retain underlying notes. No change to
refutation, lock, atomic publication or source-race refusal semantics.

Analyzer is `sec8-1`. IDs hash JSON(analyzer, code, source-declaration identity,
normalized diagnostic message, repair), then JSON(base hash, occurrence ordinal).
Declaration identity is schema-relative file, declaration kind and name. Message
normalization removes only numeric source line/column suffixes; literal values,
operation/table/column names, and predicate text remain. Rewording may invalidate
reviews conservatively; this is deliberate. Identical repeated diagnostics get
individual ordinal IDs in deterministic source order; changing their declaration
changes the shared source binding, so removal/reordering cannot transfer an old
acknowledgment across changed write contexts. Tests retain one pending review
after acknowledging only one repeat, including after line shifts.

Bindings are pure source/compiled-schema functions:
- Scalar optional-evidence column notes bind that column's lexer tokens, excluding
  locations/comments and unrelated columns. Complex optional-evidence flows fall
  through to the declaration closure.
- Restatement and role/broad-read notes bind the effective row/column rule, exact
  column type, matching independent intent, transitive dependency schemas and all
  bound programs writing those dependencies. Non-read intent includes write rules
  and field ownership. Source sites are stripped before canonical serialization.
- All other codes, including both gates, bind the containing declaration and every
  declaration named in the note; transitively include referenced declarations,
  all mutations/jobs that write a selected table, and its independent invariants.
  Helper and sibling-write bodies are therefore included. Canonical lexer tokens
  retain values and indentation but exclude comments, locations and newlines.
  This deliberately conservative closure may invalidate another note on the same
  affected resource; it never binds an independent declaration merely for sharing
  a source file. Unlocated unknown notes use an explicitly labelled whole-source
  fallback. Never bind an empty semantic slice. Old analyzer records stay readable
  but cannot acknowledge this analyzer's notes.

The advisory detectors are intentionally finite and may ask unnecessary questions:
- stale eligibility retains §16.9's same-resource/column identity extraction but
  admits any literal mutable enum destination other than its default creation
  value, including rejection/inspection; true approval flags retain the existing
  `approv`/`authoriz` substrings. No claim that every destination needs rechecking.
- approval-not-enforced: a true `approv`/`authoriz` Boolean is authored, and a
  same-table enum updater does not inspect it in its dominating resolved requires.
- edit-after-approval: a media column on an approval/stage-bearing table, or a
  payload inspected by another literal-enum/approval-flag decision, is edited
  without positive equality to the same target's default creation state. A
  negative cancelled-state check does not prove editing precedes approval. This
  does not claim arbitrary approvals or alternative enforcement are unsupported.
- child presence: an authored media-child creator plus a direct parent reference
  and a parent enum updater, without a positive dominating `exists child` conjunct
  keyed to that exact parent reference/target. Negation, another parent and late
  requires do not suppress. Text field substrings photo/image/scan/sketch/url are
  explicitly name-based. This asks for spec intent, never implies a gate.
- missed/overdue/expired literal destinations ask about server-clock enforcement;
  dependent windows compare written Time arguments bounded against now with
  uninspected referenced Time columns named due/deadline/until/expire. Calendar
  questions recognize integer `Nd` literals divisible by 365, N >= 365. They make
  no calendar inference. Unsupported affine forms remain outside these heuristics.
- reusable guards: a dominating Boolean/enum check on a related table, or Boolean
  check on the transitioned table, is not assigned by that operation. This is a
  question about reuse, not an assertion that every guard must be consumed.

Qualification lanes/findings: 007 F2; 008 F1; 011 F1; 013 F1; 015 F1/F2;
016 F1; 029 F1; 031 F1; 033 F1; 036 F1/F2; 048 F1. Frozen source hashes,
three-mode outcomes and the exact corpus manifest belong to the SEC8 report;
no generic claim that advice has prevented an exploit. Gate counts are separated
from violations; every newly gated original project requires individual inspection.

Second-review corrections: reverse writer dependencies use compiled IR, including
helper-expanded writes; grouped column advice binds every represented column,
not only its lead column. Concrete evaluation reports contextual principal/string
coercion gaps and records every visited policy-unknown result with target/site.
Creator initialization reports omitted boolean projections at tuple/population
cuts. Only a status/stage/state enum or approval/authorization Boolean default can suppress
edit-after-approval advice; an unrelated default-valued field cannot. Overdue-clock
suppression requires an ordered server-now comparison on the transitioned row.

Third-review corrections: edit suppression recognizes only default equality on
`status`, `stage`, `state`, or an `approv`/`authoriz` Boolean; arbitrary enums and
Booleans cannot stand in for workflow approval. Unknown lifecycle spellings stay
advisory. Generated initialization checks every target's committed candidate row
count before retaining it: a candidate exceeding two rows on any target is declined
with an explicit coverage gap, even for mixed populated/empty targets or a
multi-insert creator. This is exploration pruning, never runtime admission.

### 17.6 Measurement-driven history corrections

//! A family command expands to current individual, source-bound records.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#18-wave-26-security-decisions-dx9 — review questions lead, and gates never enter the audience family.
use super::*;
use snapback4_lang::Diagnostic;

pub(super) fn audience(code: &str) -> bool {
    !gated(code) && matches!(code, "N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE" | "N_ROLE_WIDE_READ" | "N_ROLE_WIDE_WRITE" | "N_BROAD_READ")
}
pub(super) fn family(notes: &[Diagnostic], identities: &[(String,String,String)]) -> Option<String> {
    let mut members = notes.iter().zip(identities).filter(|(note,_)| audience(&note.code))
        .map(|(_, (id,source,_))| (id,source)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    if members.is_empty() { return None; }
    members.sort();
    Some(format!("audience:{}", hash(&serde_json::to_vec(&(ANALYZER,members)).unwrap())))
}
pub(in crate::verbs) fn count(dispositions: &[Disposition]) -> usize {
    let audience = dispositions.iter().any(|d| !d.acknowledged && d.family.is_some());
    usize::from(audience) + dispositions.iter().filter(|d| !d.acknowledged && d.family.is_none()
        && crate::note_rank::tier(&d.code)==crate::note_rank::Tier::Review).count()
}
pub(in crate::verbs) fn print(dispositions: &[Disposition], notes: &[Diagnostic]) {print!("{}",render(dispositions,notes));}
pub(in crate::verbs) fn render(dispositions: &[Disposition], notes: &[Diagnostic]) -> String {
    use std::fmt::Write;
    let mut output=String::new();
    let mut ordered=notes.iter().zip(dispositions).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.004#165-review-clarification-exact-detector-and-command-boundaries — grouping must preserve gate/authority priority.
    ordered.sort_by_key(|(n,_)|(crate::note_rank::summary_order(&n.code),n.site.file.clone(),n.site.line,n.site.col));
    let members=ordered.iter().filter(|(_,d)|d.family.is_some()&&!d.acknowledged).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let mut family_output=String::new();
    if let Some((_,first))=members.first().copied() {
        let codes=members.iter().map(|(n,_)|n.code.as_str()).collect::<std::collections::BTreeSet<_>>().into_iter().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        writeln!(&mut family_output,"decision audience ({codes}): Are these {} row/column audiences, roles and lifecycle states intended?",members.len()).unwrap();
        for (n,_) in &members {
            // The expanded branch cross-product stays in JSON. One question
            // names the source subject without repeating every equivalent path.
            let subject = n.message.split(" matches or weakens").next().unwrap_or(&n.message)
                .split(": ").next().unwrap_or(&n.message);
            writeln!(&mut family_output,"  ? {}: Is {} intended?",n.site,subject.trim_end_matches('.')).unwrap();
        }
        writeln!(&mut family_output,"  Intended: snapback4 check --ack-security {} --reason '<spec rationale>'",first.family.as_deref().unwrap()).unwrap();
        writeln!(&mut family_output,"  Narrower: restrict the listed rules; declare independent intent; run snapback4 test. Full audiences: check --json.").unwrap();
    }
    let mut family_printed=false;
    for (n,d) in ordered {
        if d.acknowledged { writeln!(&mut output,"security reviewed {} {}: {}",d.id,d.code,d.reason.as_deref().unwrap_or("")).unwrap(); continue; }
        if d.family.is_some() {
            if !family_printed {output.push_str(&family_output);family_printed=true;}
            continue;
        }
        if crate::note_rank::tier(&n.code)!=crate::note_rank::Tier::Review { continue; }
        let subject=n.message.split(": ").next().unwrap_or(&n.message);
        let concise=match n.code.as_str() {
            "N_COLUMN_RESULT_EXPOSURE"=>format!("{subject}: should operation results/refusals expose protected or projected-out columns to every admitted caller"),
            _=>n.message.trim_end_matches(['.', '?']).to_owned(),
        };
        let concise=concise.replace("insert/upsert", "insert, or edit an existing row via upsert");
        let question=concise.trim_end_matches(['.', '?']);
        writeln!(&mut output,"decision {} at {}: {question}; is this intended?",d.code,d.site).unwrap();
        if let Some(witness)=&d.witness {writeln!(&mut output,"  Witness: {}",witness["summary"].as_str().unwrap_or("concrete campaign behavior; check --json for replay")).unwrap();}
        else if matches!(n.code.as_str(),"N_APPROVAL_NOT_ENFORCED"|"N_ARGUMENT_GRANT"|"N_INSERT_WRITE_INTENT"|"N_DERIVED_WRITE_AUTHORITY") {writeln!(&mut output,"  Witness: none obtained in this bounded campaign; this remains a specification question").unwrap();}
        let repair=match n.code.as_str() {
            "N_COLUMN_RESULT_EXPOSURE"=>"restrict result/refusal audiences with column rules and independent intent; run snapback4 test",
            "N_ARGUMENT_GRANT"=>"validate the recipient's current role and relationship; caller authority alone is insufficient",
            _=>n.rewrite.as_str(),
        };
        writeln!(&mut output,"  Repair: {repair}").unwrap();
        writeln!(&mut output,"  Intended: snapback4 check --ack-security {} --reason '<spec rationale>'{}",d.id,if d.gated {" (required before check can pass)"}else{""}).unwrap();
    }
    output
}

#[cfg(test)]mod tests {
 use super::*;
 #[test]fn authority_and_gates_precede_one_complete_audience_family(){
  let codes=["N_BROAD_READ","N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION","N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE","N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE"];
  let notes=codes.iter().map(|code|Diagnostic::new(&Default::default(),*code,"subject: review authority","repair enforcement")).collect::<Vec<_>>();
  let dispositions=codes.iter().map(|code|Disposition{family:audience(code).then(||"audience:family".into()),id:(*code).into(),code:(*code).into(),site:"schema.q:1:1".into(),gated:gated(code),acknowledged:false,reason:None,witness:None,scope:"test".into()}).collect::<Vec<_>>();
  let text=render(&dispositions,&notes);
  assert!(text.find("decision N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE").unwrap()<text.find("decision N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION").unwrap(),"{text}");
  assert!(text.find("decision N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION").unwrap()<text.find("decision audience").unwrap(),"{text}");
  assert_eq!(text.matches("decision audience").count(),1,"{text}");
  assert!(text.contains("Are these 2 row/column audiences")&&text.contains("audience:family"),"{text}");
 }
 #[test]fn every_gate_is_one_decision_and_never_a_family_member(){
  for code in snapback4_lang::security::GATED_NOTE_CODES {
   assert!(!audience(code));
   let n=Diagnostic::new(&Default::default(),*code,"explicit review required","repair enforcement");
   let d=Disposition{family:None,id:"id".into(),code:(*code).into(),site:"schema.q:1:1".into(),gated:true,acknowledged:false,reason:None,witness:None,scope:"test".into()};
   let text=render(&[d],&[n]);assert_eq!(text.matches(code).count(),1,"{text}");assert!(text.contains("is this intended?")&&text.contains("Repair:")&&text.contains("Intended:"));
  }
  for code in ["N_DERIVED_WRITE_AUTHORITY","N_CALLER_TIME","N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE","N_APPROVAL_NOT_ENFORCED","E_SECURITY_REVIEW"] {assert!(!audience(code));}
 }
}

//! Individually justified source-bound dispositions; never proof or authority.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#162-narrow-gates-and-individual-dispositions — bulk compiler-note review cannot clear security gates.
use super::*;
use serde::{Serialize,Deserialize};
use sha2::{Digest,Sha256};
const ANALYZER:&str="sec11-1";
mod binding;
mod decisions;
#[derive(Serialize,Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
struct File{version:u32,entries:BTreeMap<String,Entry>}
#[derive(Serialize,Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
struct Entry{sources:String,analyzer:String,reason:String,#[serde(default,skip_serializing_if="Option::is_none")] witness:Option<serde_json::Value>}
#[derive(Clone,Serialize)]
pub(super) struct Disposition{pub family:Option<String>,pub id:String,pub code:String,pub site:String,pub gated:bool,pub acknowledged:bool,pub reason:Option<String>,pub scope:String,pub witness:Option<serde_json::Value>}
pub(super) fn gated(code:&str)->bool{snapback4_lang::security::gated_note(code)}
fn hash(bytes:&[u8])->String{format!("{:x}",Sha256::digest(bytes))}
fn digest(s:&str)->bool{s.len()==64&&s.bytes().all(|b|b.is_ascii_hexdigit())}
pub(super) fn review(project:&Project,sources:&[(String,String)],notes:&[snapback4_lang::Diagnostic],ack:Option<(&str,&str)>)->Result<Vec<Disposition>,String>{
    review_inner(project,sources,notes,ack,false,&[])
}
/// Read-only decisions for a caller-validated immutable saved-source selection.
pub(super) fn review_snapshot(project:&Project,sources:&[(String,String)],notes:&[snapback4_lang::Diagnostic])->Result<Vec<Disposition>,String>{
    review_inner(project,sources,notes,None,true,&[])
}
pub(super) fn review_evidence(project:&Project,sources:&[(String,String)],notes:&[snapback4_lang::Diagnostic],ack:Option<(&str,&str)>,evidence:&[Option<serde_json::Value>])->Result<Vec<Disposition>,String>{
    review_inner(project,sources,notes,ack,false,evidence)
}
fn review_inner(project:&Project,sources:&[(String,String)],notes:&[snapback4_lang::Diagnostic],ack:Option<(&str,&str)>,snapshot:bool,evidence:&[Option<serde_json::Value>])->Result<Vec<Disposition>,String>{
    if !snapshot&&project.sources().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?!=sources{return Err("Quarry sources changed during security review; rerun check".into());}
    let identities=binding::identities(notes,sources);
    let path=project.schema_dir().join("security-reviews.json");
    // Keep the lock inode in ignored generated output. Do not unlink a lock
    // while another process can be waiting on that inode. Kernel ownership
    // releases it on exit, including an interrupted writer.
    let _writer_lock=if ack.is_some(){
        let directory=project.schema_dir().join("generated");
        std::fs::create_dir_all(&directory).map_err(|e|format!("cannot create security review lock directory: {e}"))?;
        let lock=std::fs::OpenOptions::new().create(true).truncate(false).read(true).write(true).open(directory.join("security-reviews.lock")).map_err(|e|format!("cannot open security review lock: {e}"))?;
        lock.lock().map_err(|e|format!("cannot acquire security review lock: {e}"))?;Some(lock)
    }else{None};
    if !snapshot&&project.sources().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?!=sources{return Err("Quarry sources changed while waiting for security review; rerun check".into());}
    let mut file:File=match std::fs::read(&path){
        Ok(bytes)=>serde_json::from_slice(&bytes).map_err(|_|"invalid snapback/security-reviews.json; expected version 1 with per-note source/analyzer/reason records".to_string())?,
        Err(e) if e.kind()==std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound=>File{version:1,entries:BTreeMap::new()},
        Err(e)=>return Err(format!("cannot read security dispositions: {e}")),
    };
    if file.version!=1||file.entries.iter().any(|(key,e)|!digest(key)||!digest(&e.sources)||e.reason.trim().is_empty()||e.analyzer.is_empty()){return Err("invalid security disposition version/digest/reason".into());}
    if let Some((key,reason))=ack{
        if reason.trim().is_empty(){return Err("--reason needs the specification rationale for this individual note".into());}
        // Validate the complete batch against the same captured source/note set.
        // Nothing is published when even one member is stale or unknown.
        let mut selected=std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
        for key in key.split(',').map(str::trim) {
            if key.starts_with("audience:") {
                let Some(family)=decisions::family(notes,&identities) else {return Err("no current advisory audience family; run check --review --json".into());};
                if family==key {
                    selected.extend(notes.iter().enumerate().filter(|(_,note)|decisions::audience(&note.code)).map(|(i,_)|i));
                } else {
                    let history: BTreeMap<String,Vec<(String,String)>>=std::fs::read(project.schema_dir().join("generated/security-families.json")).ok().and_then(|b|serde_json::from_slice(&b).ok()).unwrap_or_default();
                    let members=history.get(key).ok_or("stale or unknown audience family: rerun check --review; existing individual decisions are preserved")?;
                    for (id,fingerprint) in members {
                        let index=identities.iter().enumerate().find(|(i,(current,binding,_))|current==id&&binding==fingerprint&&decisions::audience(&notes[*i].code)).map(|(i,_)|i).ok_or("stale audience family member: a relevant rule changed or disappeared; no decisions recorded; run check --review")?;
                        selected.insert(index);
                    }
                }
            } else {
                let index=identities.iter().position(|(id,_,_)|id==key).ok_or_else(||format!("--ack-security {key} is stale, belongs to another source selection, or is not a decision ID. Use securityReviews[].id from `check --review --json` (not a diagnostic code or invariant site); repeated --ack-security ID or comma-separated IDs share one --reason"))?;
                selected.insert(index);
            }
        }
        for index in selected {
            let (id,fingerprint,_)=&identities[index];
            file.entries.insert(id.clone(),Entry{sources:fingerprint.clone(),analyzer:ANALYZER.into(),reason:reason.trim().into(),witness:evidence.get(index).cloned().flatten()});
        }
        let temporary=path.with_extension(format!("json.{}.tmp",std::process::id()));
        let result=std::fs::write(&temporary,serde_json::to_vec_pretty(&file).unwrap()).and_then(|_|{
            if project.sources()?!=sources{return Err(std::io::Error::other("Quarry sources changed before disposition publication; rerun check"));}
            std::fs::rename(&temporary,&path)
        });
        if let Err(e)=result{let _=std::fs::remove_file(&temporary);return Err(format!("cannot record security disposition: {e}"));}
    }
    let family=decisions::family(notes,&identities);
    if !snapshot && let Some(family)=&family {
        let path=project.schema_dir().join("generated/security-families.json");
        let mut history:BTreeMap<String,Vec<(String,String)>>=std::fs::read(&path).ok().and_then(|b|serde_json::from_slice(&b).ok()).unwrap_or_default();
        history.insert(family.clone(),identities.iter().zip(notes).filter(|(_,n)|decisions::audience(&n.code)).map(|((id,binding,_),_)|(id.clone(),binding.clone())).collect());
        let temporary=path.with_extension(format!("{}.tmp",std::process::id()));
        let _=std::fs::create_dir_all(path.parent().unwrap());
        if std::fs::write(&temporary,serde_json::to_vec(&history).unwrap()).is_ok(){let _=std::fs::rename(&temporary,&path);}let _=std::fs::remove_file(temporary);
    }
    Ok(notes.iter().zip(identities).enumerate().map(|(i,(n,(key,fingerprint,scope)))|{let entry=file.entries.get(&key).filter(|e|e.sources==fingerprint&&e.analyzer==ANALYZER);
        Disposition{family:decisions::audience(&n.code).then(||family.clone()).flatten(),id:key,code:n.code.clone(),site:n.site.to_string(),gated:gated(&n.code),acknowledged:entry.is_some(),scope,reason:entry.map(|e|e.reason.clone()),witness:entry.and_then(|e|e.witness.clone()).or_else(||evidence.get(i).cloned().flatten())}
    }).collect())
}
pub(super) fn pending(dispositions:&[Disposition])->bool{dispositions.iter().any(|d|d.gated&&!d.acknowledged)}
pub(super) use decisions::{print,render,count as decision_count};

 assert!(review(&p,&sources,&notes,Some((&key,"  "))).is_err());assert!(review(&p,&sources,&notes,Some(("unknown","intended"))).is_err());
 let ds=review(&p,&sources,&notes,Some((&key,"the specification allows optional draft evidence"))).unwrap();assert!(ds[0].acknowledged);assert!(!ds[1].acknowledged);assert!(pending(&ds));

 std::fs::write(p.schema_dir().join("schema.q"),"query q():\n  return false\n").unwrap();assert!(review(&p,&sources,&notes,Some((&key,"intended"))).is_err());assert!(!review(&p,&p.sources().unwrap(),&notes,None).unwrap()[0].acknowledged);
 std::fs::write(&path,"{}").unwrap();assert!(review(&p,&p.sources().unwrap(),&notes,None).is_err());std::fs::remove_dir_all(root).unwrap();
}
#[test]fn repeated_advisories_are_individually_reviewed_and_survive_comments(){
 let root=std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("sb4-sec8-advisory-{}",std::process::id()));std::fs::create_dir_all(root.join("snapback")).unwrap();let _scratch=Scratch(root.clone());let p=Project{root};

 let ds=review(&p,&p.sources().unwrap(),&notes,None).unwrap();assert_ne!(ds[0].id,ds[1].id);
 let ds=review(&p,&p.sources().unwrap(),&notes,Some((&ds[0].id,"Specification allows this audience"))).unwrap();assert!(ds[0].acknowledged);assert!(!ds[1].acknowledged);assert!(!pending(&ds));
 std::fs::write(p.schema_dir().join("schema.q"),format!("-- comment\n{text}")).unwrap();n.site.line+=1;

#[cfg(test)] mod family_tests {
 use super::*;
 #[test] fn audience_command_records_current_members_but_never_gates_or_changed_families() {
  let root=std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("sb4-dx9-family-{}",std::process::id()));
  std::fs::create_dir_all(root.join("snapback")).unwrap();let _scratch=super::tests::Scratch(root.clone());
  let p=Project{root};std::fs::write(p.schema_dir().join("schema.q"),"query q():\n  return true\n").unwrap();let sources=p.sources().unwrap();
  let n=|code:&str,message:&str|snapback4_lang::Diagnostic::new(&Default::default(),code,message,"confirm");
  let notes=vec![n("N_ROLE_WIDE_READ","notes.read: audience A"),n("N_ROLE_WIDE_WRITE","notes.write: audience B"),n("N_PRESENCE_LOOSENING","gate")];
  let ds=review(&p,&sources,&notes,None).unwrap();let family=ds[0].family.clone().unwrap();assert_eq!(ds[1].family.as_ref(),Some(&family));assert!(ds[2].family.is_none());
  let recorded=review(&p,&sources,&notes,Some((&family,"The specification admits both audiences"))).unwrap();
  assert!(recorded[0].acknowledged&&recorded[1].acknowledged);assert!(!recorded[2].acknowledged);assert!(pending(&recorded));
  let mut changed=notes.clone();changed.push(n("N_BROAD_READ","extra.read: another audience"));
  let retained=review(&p,&sources,&changed,Some((&family,"old decision"))).unwrap();
  assert!(retained[0].acknowledged&&retained[1].acknowledged);assert!(!retained[2].acknowledged&&!retained[3].acknowledged);
  assert!(review(&p,&sources,&notes,Some((&family,"  "))).is_err());
 }
}
