//! Stable semantic review bindings. Complex flow notes conservatively bind
//! their declaration dependency/writer closure, never the whole source set.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#173-presentation-and-review-identity — review survives independent edits.
use super::*;
use std::collections::BTreeSet;
use snapback4_lang::lexer::{lex,Kind};
#[derive(Clone)]
struct Declaration{file:String,line:u32,end:u32,name:String,kind:String,tokens:Vec<(String,String)>}
fn declarations(sources:&[(String,String)])->Vec<Declaration>{
    let mut out=Vec::new();
    for (file,text) in sources{
        let (tokens,_)=lex(file,text);
        let starts=tokens.iter().enumerate().filter(|(_,t)|t.site.col==1&&t.kind==Kind::Word).map(|(i,_)|i).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        for (n,start) in starts.iter().enumerate(){
            let end=starts.get(n+1).copied().unwrap_or(tokens.len());let ts=&tokens[*start..end];
            let Some(name)=ts.get(1).filter(|t|t.kind==Kind::Word)else{continue;};
            out.push(Declaration{file:file.clone(),line:ts[0].site.line,end:tokens.get(end).map_or(u32::MAX,|t|t.site.line),name:name.value.clone(),kind:ts[0].value.clone(),tokens:ts.iter().filter(|t|!matches!(t.kind,Kind::Newline|Kind::Eof)).map(|t|(format!("{:?}",t.kind),t.value.clone())).collect()});
        }
    }
    out
}
fn words(text:&str)->BTreeSet<&str>{text.split(|c:char|!c.is_alphanumeric()&&c!='_').filter(|s|!s.is_empty()).collect()}
fn stable_message(text:&str)->String{
    // Source sites in detector prose are evidence, not review identity.
    text.split_inclusive(char::is_whitespace).map(|chunk|{
        let word=chunk.trim_end_matches(char::is_whitespace);
        let suffix=&chunk[word.len()..];
        let parts=word.split(':').collect::<Vec<_>>();
        let normalized=if parts.len()>=3&&parts[parts.len()-2].parse::<u32>().is_ok()&&parts.last().unwrap().trim_end_matches([',',';',')']).parse::<u32>().is_ok(){parts[..parts.len()-2].join(":")}else{word.into()};
        format!("{normalized}{suffix}")
    }).collect()

}
fn strip_sites(value:&mut serde_json::Value){match value{
    serde_json::Value::Object(fields)=>{
        let metadata=fields.get("site").is_some_and(|site|site.is_string()||site.as_object().is_some_and(|site|site.len()==3&&site.contains_key("file")&&site.contains_key("line")&&site.contains_key("col")));
        if metadata{fields.remove("site");}
        for value in fields.values_mut(){strip_sites(value);}
    },
    serde_json::Value::Array(values)=>for value in values{strip_sites(value)},_=>{}
}}
pub(super) fn identities(notes:&[snapback4_lang::Diagnostic],sources:&[(String,String)])->Vec<(String,String,String)>{
    let decls=declarations(sources);
    let bound=snapback4_lang::compile(sources);
    let mut occurrences=BTreeMap::<String,usize>::new();
    notes.iter().map(|note|{
        let owner=decls.iter().find(|d|d.file==note.site.file&&d.line<=note.site.line&&note.site.line<d.end);
        let subject=owner.map(|d|format!("{}:{}:{}",d.file,d.kind,d.name)).unwrap_or_else(||note.site.file.clone());
        let base=hash(&serde_json::to_vec(&(ANALYZER,&note.code,&subject,stable_message(&note.message),stable_message(&note.rewrite))).unwrap());
        let ordinal=occurrences.entry(base.clone()).or_default();let key=hash(&serde_json::to_vec(&(&base,*ordinal)).unwrap());*ordinal+=1;
        let mentioned=words(&note.message);
        let mut selected=decls.iter().enumerate().filter(|(_,d)|mentioned.contains(d.name.as_str())||owner.is_some_and(|o|std::ptr::eq(o,*d))).map(|(i,_)|i).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
        // Optional-column advice is exactly about that declaration's type and
        // rule; unrelated columns in the same table are not dependencies.
        let column=note.message.split_whitespace().next().and_then(|s|s.split_once('.')).filter(|(_,c)|!c.contains(':'));
        let precise=if note.code=="N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE"{column.and_then(|(table,column)|{
            sources.iter().find_map(|(file,text)|{
                let (doc,_)=snapback4_lang::parse_document(file,text);
                let c=doc.tables.iter().find(|t|t.name==table)?.columns.iter().find(|c|c.name==column)?;
                let (tokens,_)=lex(file,text);let tokens=tokens.iter().filter(|t|t.site.line==c.site.line&&!matches!(t.kind,Kind::Newline|Kind::Eof)).map(|t|(format!("{:?}",t.kind),t.value.clone())).collect::<Vec<_>>();
                Some(serde_json::to_vec(&(file,table,column,tokens)).unwrap())
            })
        })}else{None};
        let rule_binding=if matches!(note.code.as_str(),"N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE"|"N_ROLE_WIDE_READ"|"N_BROAD_READ"){
            let label=note.message.split_whitespace().next().unwrap_or("").trim_end_matches(':');
            label.split_once('.').and_then(|(table,part)|{
                let t=bound.schema.tables.get(table)?;
                let column=t.columns.contains_key(part).then_some(part);
                let effect=if column.is_some(){note.message.split_whitespace().nth(1).unwrap_or("read").trim_end_matches(':')}else{part};
                let mut columns=column.into_iter().collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
                if note.code=="N_ROLE_WIDE_READ"&&let Some(group)=note.message.split("protected columns [").nth(1).and_then(|s|s.split(']').next()){
                    columns.extend(group.split(',').map(str::trim).filter(|c|t.columns.contains_key(*c)));
                }
                let column_bindings=columns.iter().map(|c|(*c,serde_json::json!({"type":t.columns.get(*c),"read":t.column_reads.get(*c),"owner":t.written_by.get(*c)}))).collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>();
                let mut value=serde_json::json!({"rowRead":t.rules.read,"columns":column_bindings,"effect":effect,
                    "invariants":bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.table==table&&(i.column.is_none()&&column.is_none()||i.column.as_deref().is_some_and(|c|columns.contains(c)))&&(i.target==effect||i.target=="write")).collect::<Vec<_>>()});
                if effect!="read"{value["writeRules"]=serde_json::to_value(&t.rules).unwrap();value["owners"]=serde_json::to_value(&t.written_by).unwrap();}
                strip_sites(&mut value);
                let encoded=value.to_string();
                let mut dependencies=bound.schema.tables.keys().filter(|name|name.as_str()!=table&&encoded.contains(&format!("\"{name}\""))).cloned().collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
                loop{let old=dependencies.clone();for name in &old{let serialized=serde_json::to_string(&bound.schema.tables[name]).unwrap();for candidate in bound.schema.tables.keys(){if candidate.as_str()!=table&&serialized.contains(&format!("\"{candidate}\"")){dependencies.insert(candidate.clone());}}}if dependencies==old{break;}}
                value["dependencies"]=serde_json::to_value(dependencies.iter().map(|name|(name,&bound.schema.tables[name])).collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>()).unwrap();
                let mut writers=serde_json::to_value(bound.programs.iter().filter(|p|snapback4_core::ir::write_tables(&p.program).iter().any(|(table,_)|dependencies.contains(table))).map(|p|&p.program).collect::<Vec<_>>()).unwrap();strip_sites(&mut writers);value["dependencyWriters"]=writers;
                Some(serde_json::to_vec(&value).unwrap())
            })
        }else{None};
        let scope=if precise.is_some(){"exact column declaration"}else if rule_binding.is_some(){"effective rule/column and authority dependency closure"}else if selected.is_empty(){"whole-source fallback: unlocated/unknown subject"}else{"source declaration and dependency/writer closure (conservative)"};
        let fingerprint=if let Some(bytes)=precise.or(rule_binding){hash(&bytes)}else{
            // Forward names include helper bodies and referenced schemas.
            // Reverse writers include newly added mutators of a trusted grant.
            loop{
                let before=selected.clone();
                let names=selected.iter().map(|i|decls[*i].name.as_str()).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
                let dependencies=selected.iter().flat_map(|i|decls[*i].tokens.iter().filter(|(k,_)|k=="Word").map(|(_,v)|v.as_str())).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
                for (i,d) in decls.iter().enumerate(){
                    let module=std::path::Path::new(&d.file).file_stem().and_then(|s|s.to_str()).unwrap_or("");
                    let operation=if module=="schema"{d.name.clone()}else{format!("{module}.{}",d.name)};
                    let writer=matches!(d.kind.as_str(),"mutation"|"job")&&bound.programs.iter().find(|p|p.program.name==operation).is_some_and(|p|snapback4_core::ir::write_tables(&p.program).iter().any(|(table,_)|names.contains(table.as_str())));
                    let intent=d.kind=="invariant"&&names.contains(d.name.as_str());
                    if dependencies.contains(d.name.as_str())||writer||intent{selected.insert(i);}
                }
                if selected==before{break;}
            }
            if selected.is_empty(){
                // Unknown source ownership is not an empty authority binding.
                hash(&serde_json::to_vec(sources).unwrap())
            }else{
                let mut binding=selected.iter().map(|i|{let d=&decls[*i];(&d.file,&d.kind,&d.name,&d.tokens)}).collect::<Vec<_>>();binding.sort();
                hash(&serde_json::to_vec(&binding).unwrap())
            }
        };
        (key,fingerprint,scope.into())
    }).collect()
}

#[cfg(test)]mod tests{
use super::*;
use std::collections::BTreeSet;
fn note(line:u32,code:&str,message:&str)->snapback4_lang::Diagnostic{snapback4_lang::Diagnostic::new(&snapback4_lang::Site::new("schema.q",line,3),code,message,"review")}
fn ids(source:&str,notes:&[snapback4_lang::Diagnostic])->Vec<(String,String,String)>{identities(notes,&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())])}
const SOURCE:&str="table docs:\n  photo: image <=10000 ?\n  title: text <=100\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- allow\n  online only\nmutation add(photo:image <=10000 ?):\n  return insert docs {photo,title:'x'}\nquery unrelated():\n  return true\n";
#[test]fn independent_edits_and_comments_preserve_column_decisions(){
 let n=note(2,"N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE","docs.photo permits omitted or empty media evidence");let a=ids(SOURCE,&[n.clone()]);
 let changed=SOURCE.replace("title: text <=100","title: text <=200").replace("return true","return false");assert_eq!(a,ids(&changed,&[n.clone()]));
 let shifted=format!("-- a comment\n\n{SOURCE}");assert_eq!(a,ids(&shifted,&[note(4,&n.code,&n.message)]));
 let changed=SOURCE.replace("photo: image <=10000 ?","photo: image <=20000 ?");assert_ne!(a[0].1,ids(&changed,&[n])[0].1);
}
#[test]fn flow_decisions_bind_helpers_and_new_writers_but_not_independent_queries(){
 let n=note(8,"N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE","add: optional photo creates docs.photo");let a=ids(SOURCE,&[n.clone()]);
 assert_eq!(a,ids(&SOURCE.replace("return true","return false"),&[n.clone()]));
 let changed=format!("{SOURCE}mutation clear(doc:docs):\n  update docs[doc] {{photo:null}}\n");assert_ne!(a[0].1,ids(&changed,&[n.clone()])[0].1);
 let changed=SOURCE.replace("title:'x'","title:'y'");assert_ne!(a[0].1,ids(&changed,&[n])[0].1);
}
#[test]fn repeated_writes_keep_individual_ids_across_line_shifts(){
 let mut a=note(8,"N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE","add: optional photo creates docs.photo");let mut b=a.clone();b.site.line=9;
 let original=ids(SOURCE,&[a.clone(),b.clone()]);assert_ne!(original[0].0,original[1].0);
 a.site.line+=2;b.site.line+=2;assert_eq!(original,ids(&format!("-- comment\n\n{SOURCE}"),&[a,b]));
}
#[test]fn helpers_that_write_a_trusted_table_are_reverse_dependencies(){
 let s=SOURCE.to_owned()+"fn patch(doc:docs,title:text <=100):\n  update docs[doc] {title}\n  return null\nmutation edit(doc:docs):\n  return patch(doc,'a')\n";
 let n=note(8,"N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE","add: optional photo creates docs.photo");
 assert_ne!(ids(&s,&[n.clone()])[0].1,ids(&s.replace("patch(doc,'a')","patch(doc,'b')"),&[n])[0].1);
}
#[test]fn grouped_column_questions_bind_the_nonfirst_column(){
 let s="table docs:\n  a:text <=100\n  b:text <=100\n  read <- allow\n  online only\n";
 let n=note(2,"N_ROLE_WIDE_READ","docs.a read: protected columns [a, b] admit staff for every state");
 assert_ne!(ids(s,&[n.clone()])[0].1,ids(&s.replace("b:text <=100","b:text <=200"),&[n])[0].1);
}

#[test]fn review_identity_preserves_whitespace_inside_literal_values(){
 let a=note(8,"N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE","add: role = 'two  words'");let b=note(8,"N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE","add: role = 'two words'");assert_ne!(ids(SOURCE,&[a])[0].0,ids(SOURCE,&[b])[0].0);
}

#[test]fn an_application_column_named_site_is_not_source_metadata(){
 let s="table docs:\n  a:text <=100\n  site:text <=100\n  read <- allow\n  online only\n";
 let n=note(2,"N_ROLE_WIDE_READ","docs.a read: protected columns [a, site] admit staff for every state");assert_ne!(ids(s,&[n.clone()])[0].1,ids(&s.replace("site:text <=100","site:text <=200"),&[n])[0].1);
}

}

 `{ id: uploaded.asset.id }` through a mutation, then render with `useAsset`.
-Image capture in a browser uses the same upload path:
+Parallel uploads can exhaust server admission slots (`E_RATE_LIMIT`, HTTP 429).
+Upload a batch sequentially. The client retries explicit retryable admission
+refusals with bounded backoff and Retry-After; if it still refuses, keep the
+selected bytes and retry after that delay. Other failures are not automatically
+retried because acceptance may be uncertain.
+
+Expo starters include `src/UploadPicker.tsx`: a file input on web and an image
+library picker on native, with pending/error feedback. Its `onUploaded(id)`
+callback places the id through your generated mutation; selection alone does
+not save a row. Image capture in a browser uses the same upload path:
 
 ```tsx
 <input type="file" accept="image/jpeg,image/png,image/webp" capture="environment"

### Source diagnostics without an existence oracle

Extend LLP 3000.036.002 §15.2's N_LOOKUP_FILTERED advice to required reads
and reads of written tables. A later write and an asserted `! else CODE` do
not prove read authority. Existing key/flow proofs still suppress advice,
including prior successful exact reads and deliberately public projections.
One note names the source lookup, authored hiding rule, declaration and the
null/custom-refusal consequence; duplicate N_LOOKUP_HIDDEN notes at that
site are avoided (the more specific N_LOOKUP_HIDDEN takes precedence). This is missing-proof advice, not a claim every caller
is refused. Program `exists` and rule-witness `exists` keep distinct semantics.

For private tables with secondary unique keys, source advice explains that
an unreadable conflicting row uses generic E_RULE (or an undisclosed write
target miss), even when the unique declaration has `else CODE`. The named
constraint applies only when the conflict can be disclosed. Do not return
the declared code only on hidden conflicts: that would violate LLP 3000 §7
and LLP 3000.036.002 §15.2. Public E_RULE repair text, if changed, is identical
for ordinary rule rejection and hidden collision. No row, key, actual hiding
clause or hidden-dependent meter enters the public refusal. Rewrites propose
a minimal readable key projection or operator-mediated ownership/claim flow,
never broader disclosure of private rows. Paired hidden/missing lookup tests assert equality of public diagnostics and
logical meters. Hidden/ordinary write-refusal tests compare public refusals
and receipts; write replies expose no meter.

pub enum ColumnType {
    Id,
    Principal,
    Text { max: u32 },
    Bytes { max: u32 },
    Image { max: u32, #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::ops::Not::not")] once: bool },
    Video { max: u32 },
    // @ref LLP 3000.038#1 — an empty allow-list is the default: every verifiable type.
    File { max: u32, #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")] types: Vec<String> },
    Int,
    // @ref LLP 3000.040#13-representation — a new tag fails closed on older decoders; `Int` keeps its bytes.
    IntRange { #[serde(default = "crate::schema::int_floor", skip_serializing_if = "crate::schema::is_int_floor")] min: i64, #[serde(default = "crate::schema::int_ceiling", skip_serializing_if = "crate::schema::is_int_ceiling")] max: i64 },
    Enum { variants: Vec<String> },
    Decimal { scale: u8 },
    Money { currency: String, scale: u8 },
    Bool,
    Time,
    Ref { table: String },
    Json { max_bytes: u32 },
    Optional(Box<ColumnType>),
    List { of: Box<ColumnType>, max: u32 },
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — a new enum tag makes older engines refuse the minimum.
    ListRange { of: Box<ColumnType>, min: u32, max: u32 },
}
impl ColumnType {
    pub fn required(&self) -> &ColumnType {
        match self {
            Self::Optional(inner) => inner,
            other => other,
        }
    }

    pub fn is_optional(&self) -> bool { matches!(self, Self::Optional(_)) }

    /// An integer slot, bounded or not: the same value, order, key and arithmetic.
    pub fn is_int(&self) -> bool { matches!(self, Self::Int | Self::IntRange { .. }) }

    /// The value's kind without a slot bound: `IntRange` reads as `Int`.
    pub fn unbounded(&self) -> ColumnType {
        match self {
            Self::IntRange { .. } => Self::Int,
            Self::Optional(inner) => Self::Optional(Box::new(inner.unbounded())),
            other => other.clone(),
        }
    }

    pub fn is_indexable(&self) -> bool {
        match self {
            Self::Image { .. } | Self::Video { .. } | Self::File { .. } | Self::List { .. } | Self::ListRange { .. } | Self::Json { .. } => false,
            Self::Optional(inner) => inner.is_indexable(),
            _ => true,
        }
    }

    /// An asset leaf's kind, byte bound and (for a file) allow-list.
    pub fn asset_leaf(&self) -> Option<(crate::assets::AssetKind, u32, &[String])> {
        use crate::assets::AssetKind;
        match self {
            Self::Image { max, .. } => Some((AssetKind::Image, *max, &[])),
            Self::Video { max } => Some((AssetKind::Video, *max, &[])),
            Self::File { max, types } => Some((AssetKind::File, *max, types)),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    pub fn contains_asset(&self) -> bool {
        match self {
            Self::Image { .. } | Self::Video { .. } | Self::File { .. } => true,
            Self::Optional(inner) | Self::List { of: inner, .. } | Self::ListRange { of: inner, .. } => inner.contains_asset(),
            _ => false,
        }
    }

#[derive(Clone,Debug,Default,Serialize)]
pub struct Campaign {#[doc(hidden)] #[serde(skip)] pub time_samples:Option<Vec<Value>>,pub evaluation_unknowns:BTreeSet<String>,pub directed_attempts:usize,pub directed_peak_worlds:usize,#[serde(skip)] pub history_limit:usize,#[serde(skip)] pub generated_fixture:bool,#[serde(skip)] pub work_remaining:Option<usize>,#[serde(skip)] pub deadline:Option<std::time::Instant>,pub paired_observer_calls:usize,pub history_observer_calls:usize,pub column_observer_calls:usize,pub operations:BTreeMap<String,usize>,pub sequences:usize,pub attempted:usize,pub admitted:usize,pub refused:usize,pub violations:Vec<Counterexample>,pub boundary_failures:Vec<String>,pub unsupported:BTreeSet<String>}
impl Campaign {
    pub fn bounded(milliseconds:u64)->Self {Self{work_remaining:Some(512),deadline:Some(std::time::Instant::now()+std::time::Duration::from_millis(milliseconds)),..Self::default()}}
    pub(super) fn reserve(&mut self,cost:usize)->bool {
        if self.exhausted(){return false;}
        if let Some(remaining)=&mut self.work_remaining {
            if *remaining<cost{*remaining=0;self.unsupported.insert("check concrete exploration work budget exhausted; remaining paths unproved; run snapback4 test for the full campaign".into());return false;}
            *remaining-=cost;
        }true
    }
    pub(super) fn exhausted(&mut self)->bool {
        if self.work_remaining==Some(0){self.unsupported.insert("check concrete exploration work budget exhausted; remaining paths unproved; run snapback4 test for the full campaign".into());return true;}
        if self.deadline.is_some_and(|deadline|std::time::Instant::now()>=deadline){self.unsupported.insert("check concrete exploration time budget exhausted; remaining paths unproved; run snapback4 test for the full campaign".into());true}else{false}
    }
}

    C::Ref{table}=>id(table),C::Text{max}=>Value::String("x".repeat((*max).min(20) as usize)),

})}
fn needed(schema:&Schema,name:&str,out:&mut BTreeSet<String>){
    if !out.insert(name.into()){return;}let Some(t)=schema.table(name)else{return};
    for m in schema.maintains.iter().filter(|m|m.target==name){needed(schema,&m.source,out);}
    for k in t.columns.values(){if let C::Ref{table}=k.required(){needed(schema,table,out);}}
    for r in super::columns::rules(t){for dep in kernel::dependencies(r){needed(schema,&dep,out);}}
}
fn snapshot(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,variant:usize)->Result<BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,String>{
    snapshot_mode(bound,inv,variant,false)
}
fn snapshot_mode(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,variant:usize,timed:bool)->Result<BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,String>{
    let mut names=BTreeSet::new();needed(&bound.schema,&inv.table,&mut names);
    if let Some(policy)=&inv.policy{for dep in kernel::dependencies(policy){needed(&bound.schema,&dep,&mut names);}}
    if let Some(mut syntax)=inv.syntax.clone(){super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut syntax,&mut |e| {
        if let ast::Expr::Read(r)|ast::Expr::Exists{read:r,..}=e {needed(&bound.schema,&r.table,&mut names);}
    });}
    if let Some(op)=&inv.operation && let Some(p)=bound.programs.iter().find(|p|&p.program.name==op){for kind in p.program.args.values(){if let ir::ArgType::Ref{table}=kind{needed(&bound.schema,table,&mut names);}}}
    let mut out=BTreeMap::new();
    let mut enum_case=variant;
    for name in names {
        let t=&bound.schema.tables[&name];
        if bound.schema.maintain_for(&name).is_some(){continue;}
        let mut fields=BTreeMap::new();fields.insert("id".into(),id(&name));
        let mut principal_index=0usize;
        for (n,k) in &t.columns{
            if n=="id"||t.engine_field(n)||t.maintained.contains_key(n){continue;}
            let sample_variant=if let C::Enum{variants}=k.required(){let n=enum_case % variants.len();enum_case/=variants.len();n}else if matches!(k.required(),C::Principal){let n=((variant>>((if name==inv.table{0}else{3})+principal_index.min(5)))&1)|(variant&4);principal_index+=1;n}else{variant};
            let value=sample(k,sample_variant)
                .ok_or_else(||format!("{name}.{n}: asset ledger/required sample is not available"))?;
            fields.insert(n.clone(),value);
        }
        if timed || t.read_lifetime_sensitive() {for (n,k) in &t.columns {if matches!(k.required(),C::Time)&&(!k.is_optional()||variant&4!=0) {
            fields.insert(n.clone(),Value::Int(NOW+if t.expire.as_ref()==Some(n){2000}else if timed{1000}else{-1000}));
        }}}
        for constraint in &t.constraints{if let Constraint::Text{field,min,..}=constraint
            && matches!(fields.get(field),Some(Value::String(_))) {fields.insert(field.clone(),Value::String("x".repeat((*min).max(1) as usize)));}}
/// no generated row reaches its observer. This is coverage, never refutation.
pub fn evaluation_gaps(bound:&Bound)->Vec<String>{
    bound.invariants.iter().filter_map(|inv|{
        let mut reasons=BTreeSet::new();
        if inv.policy.is_none(){
            if let Some(syntax)=&inv.syntax{reasons.extend(super::concrete::limitations(syntax,&bound.schema,&inv.table));}
            else{reasons.insert("no concrete predicate syntax is available".into());}

        }
        (!reasons.is_empty()).then(||format!("{} at {}: {}",inv.target_label(),inv.site,reasons.into_iter().collect::<Vec<_>>().join("; ")))
    }).collect()
}

pub fn generated_campaign(bound:&Bound,campaign:&mut Campaign,matrix:bool){
    for (name,_) in bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(_,t)|t.ephemeral.is_some()){campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("ephemeral {name}: generated memory populations and ephemeral mutation histories remain uncovered; durable campaigns use isolated hub copies"));}
    campaign.history_limit=64;campaign.generated_fixture=false;
    if !campaign.reserve(1){return;}
    match engine_with(bound,&BTreeMap::new(),Vec::new()){
        Ok(empty)=>fixture_observers(bound,&empty,campaign),
        Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("empty initial campaign unavailable: {why}"));}
    }
    campaign.history_limit=448;campaign.generated_fixture=true;
    match generated_fixture(bound,campaign){
        Ok(fixture)=>if matrix{operations(bound,&fixture,campaign)}else{fixture_observers(bound,&fixture,campaign)},
        Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("generated fixture campaign unavailable: {why}"));}
    }
    campaign.history_limit=0;campaign.generated_fixture=false;
}
/// Exercise every authored query/mutation with cross-principal candidates.
/// This is conformance exploration, not a guessed spec oracle: only declared
/// policies and replayed violations can fail security acceptance.
pub fn fixture_observers(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,campaign:&mut Campaign){
    let first=campaign.violations.len();
    column_reads::direct_fixture(bound,fixture,campaign);
    histories::initial_observers(bound,fixture,campaign);
    histories::lifecycle_observers(bound,fixture,campaign);
    column_reads::fixture(bound,fixture,campaign);
    if !campaign.generated_fixture {trusted_reads(bound,fixture,campaign);}
    histories::run(bound,fixture,campaign);
    if campaign.generated_fixture {for witness in &mut campaign.violations[first..] {
        if witness.reachability==Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace {witness.reachability=Reachability::GeneratedFixtureTrace;witness.scope="actual authored observation from a validated generated initial fixture; fixture initialization is not claimed application-reachable".into();}
    }}
}

// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#143-concrete-role-refutations-and-coupled-worlds — failed implication only orders a concrete, authored replay.
pub(super) fn direct_refutations(bound:&Bound,campaign:&mut Campaign){
    let first=campaign.violations.len();
    campaign.generated_fixture=true;
    match generated_fixture(bound,campaign){
        Ok(fixture)=>{
            column_reads::direct_fixture(bound,&fixture,campaign);
            histories::initial_observers(bound,&fixture,campaign);
        },
        Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("direct read witness unavailable: {why}"));}
    }
    for v in &mut campaign.violations[first..]{
        if v.reachability==Reachability::AuthoredFixtureTrace{
            v.reachability=Reachability::GeneratedFixtureTrace;
            v.scope="actual authored observation from an insert-admitted generated fixture; initialization is not claimed reachable from an empty application".into();
        }
    }
    campaign.generated_fixture=false;
}
pub fn operations(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,campaign:&mut Campaign){
    let first=campaign.violations.len();
    fixture_observers(bound,fixture,campaign);
    assets::run(bound,fixture,campaign);

    let has_fixture=fixtures.values().any(|r|!r.is_empty());

    let quota=(8192/eligible.max(1)).clamp(1,512);let mut remaining=8192usize;
    for p in &bound.programs{
        if !matches!(p.program.kind,ProgramKind::Query|ProgramKind::Mutation)||p.program.native{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: trusted/system/opaque operation",p.program.name));continue;}

Without explicit fixture files, generate isolated deterministic typed fixtures:
two distinct principals per discovered role and service seat, related resource,
membership and ownership rows, independent principal fields, enum alternatives,
and verified image/file uploads. Ordinary seed validation, constraints, defaults,
maintenance and reference ordering remain mandatory. Declined populations name
their coverage gap; continue with the validated empty world if no population
can be admitted. Existing explicit fixtures retain their meaning.

GeneratedFixtureTrace is distinct from AuthoredFixtureTrace: it establishes an
actual authored operation/read from a validated generated initial fixture,
**not** an application operation sequence that creates that initial fixture.
A replayed policy violation from either fixture origin fails acceptance. Seed-
only and synthetic table probes remain supplemental/non-failing. Every trace
retains the full initial rows and actual arguments; reports label this origin.
The same test-only in-memory engine path works with password-only schemas;
never open development doors or change deployed identity configuration. HTTP
authentication stays separately exercised through the existing isolated serve.
A fixture-free default test may pass its generated campaigns without pretending
that application business cases ran. Saved-source and explicit business-case
requirements retain their existing contract.

### 13.3 Coverage and bounded checking

Column update intent is spelled `invariant table.column update only P`; existing
`write` still includes insert and update. Multi-write observations may judge a
unique explicit target write when other stable writes touch different tables;
ambiguous repeated target writes and unstable policy dependencies stay unproved.
Histories interleave actors, argument alternatives, relationship changes and
successor operations. Media disclosure uses compiler-typed physical payload
witnesses; alternate-world media comparisons need a coupled verified ledger.
Role-wide branches gated by row values receive advisory N_ROLE_WIDE_READ when
the table carries private intent or media; suggest a projection/board table.
Escalation advice must constrain the assigned role by dominating argument guards
before naming it; an excluded target is not a witness.

`check` performs the sound static analysis and a bounded concrete campaign;
work exhaustion is explicit unproved coverage and points to `test`. A shared
short campaign deadline bounds exploration, including observer/world loops.
Already replayed violations remain failures; timeout never awards proof.
Memoizing exact implication input pairs is allowed; the cache is bounded,
process-local, and stores only the existing kernel's result. No assumptions
about fixture rows enter static proofs. `test` and `audit` retain the full
campaign bounds. Target cold/warm median check latency is at most five seconds
on the fifty wave-18 schemas.

Default check output is one short summary block with invariant counts and at
most five ranked actionable notes with source lines and concrete next steps.
Full reasons, individual unproved paths and complete note lists require
`--verbose` (JSON retains complete structured evidence). Refuted reachable
witnesses remain visible in compact form. Recommend `snapback4 test` when
exploration remains and `snapback4 audit` when exposures need review. The
start packet/cheatsheet teach check → test → audit as the ordinary loop.

### 13.4 Qualification

Pin before/after measurements, all fifty fixture-free campaign outcomes, the
eleven high-lane spec-intent outcomes, and the 200-schema no-false-refusal
survey. Preserve frozen lane sources. Run changed snippets/starters through
packed CLI qualification, the full requested crate/client/differential suites,
Linux qualification, affected Mac tests, wasm/ref/docs/root checks. Reports
distinguish actual caught, advisory/partial, and uncovered findings. No release
version bump, push or publication belongs to SEC4.

### 13.5 Review clarifications (revision 2)

This amendment supersedes §12.2's authored-fixture-only failure rule: both
fixture origins fail on replayed authored observations, with explicit origin in
every compact failure line, JSON, counterexample selection and count. Generated
initialization remains a trusted test assumption, not proof of empty-state
reachability. Synthetic table operations and trusted-seed-only assertions still
do not fail. `apply` prefers an authored fixture trace, then a generated fixture
trace, then supplemental witnesses; retain the shortest trace within each origin.
Qualification must analyze each new generated-origin refusal, including schemas
with no authored invariants. No generated coverage yields Proved or a zero-hole
claim. The 200-schema survey defines a false refusal as a new refusal without a
replayed violation of independently confirmed intent (invariant-free schemas
must have zero new refusals). Pin each high lane's source hash and caught/partial/
not-caught finding table, and each fixture-free campaign's result and origin.

A default fixture-free test with no saved-source selection clears `no_cases`
only after its generated campaigns execute; it always prints `not covered: no
authored business cases; generated-fixture only`. Explicit fixtures and saved
sources retain the business-case rule; `--fixture-only` keeps its meaning.

Check has a shared deterministic 512 call/world reservation budget, in addition
to a 1500 ms safety deadline. Each generated world, history replay step and
observer call reserves a unit (paired observers reserve their four calls).
Existing full-campaign caps remain upper bounds. Ordering is deterministic,
with initial authored typed read observers and column pairs before history
prefixes, then remaining synthetic worlds. The test-only operation matrix remains excluded
from check; `test` and `audit` use full campaign bounds. Exhaustion names `check
concrete exploration work budget exhausted; remaining paths unproved; run
snapback4 test for the full campaign` (or `time budget exhausted`). Counts and
complete uncovered details remain in JSON/verbose. No operation result or policy
judgment cache is added: only exact full RuleExpr implication input pairs are
memoized process-locally, bounded to 4096 entries.

The compact block always counts proved/refuted/unproved, labels supplemental
seed/synthetic evidence non-failing, and lists **all** failing invariant sites
with fixture origin and one operation. Notes sort by existing note_rank tier, then security consequence (escalation,
# Security invariants

State privacy intent separately from enforcement. Check reports proved, refuted
with a replayed counterexample, or unproved with a reason; passing finite tests
are not proofs.

```quarry
table privateNotes:
  patient: principal
  body: text 1..2000
  sealed: bool default false
  read <- .patient = viewer and .sealed = false
  insert <- .patient = viewer and .sealed = false
  online only
-- Spec: a patient may see only their own note; sealing may narrow availability.
-- Removing an accidental broad branch from the rule must not change this intent.
invariant privateNotes.read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.body read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.insert only .patient = viewer
query privateNote(note: privateNotes):
  return privateNotes[note]
mutation writePrivateNote(body: text 1..2000):
  return insert privateNotes { patient: viewer, body }
```

Run `snapback4 check` → `snapback4 test` → `snapback4 audit`. Check reports
invariant counts, five ordinary note classes and all likely-hole questions; `--verbose` retains every reason.
Answer the rule questions from the product specification before editing intent.
The restating advisory names each admitted role and its row conditions: for
example, a coach branch with no assignment check asks whether coaches should
see other coaches' athletes. For every role, answer which rows, which columns,
and which status/consent conditions the spec permits. Include derived values
and downloads: hiding a diagnosis but exposing its category can reveal it.
Write that answer as independent read intent; repair a rule that admits more.
A matching or weaker assertion may still be the right simple owner policy:
keep confirmed intent. The advisory never refuses it or changes its verdict.
Review `snapback/generated/security-invariants.q.draft`: each role gets a row
question and a question for every column, including roles absent from the rule.
No candidate is accepted automatically. Use table-read intent for a shared row audience; add
column-read intent for distinct privacy boundaries. Assess every role and field
without automatically creating one assertion per question. For each role, identify
a positive grant from the spec or a necessary described workflow before retaining
access to another person's private data. Confirm intentionally shared fields
explicitly; an omitted denial is not a product grant.
Run check, test and audit again after confirming intent;
a refutation means fix the enforcement or the independently verified spec,
without widening intent just to make the result green.

Test synthesizes two users per discovered role/seat and related ownership rows
without fixture files, including password-only apps. These private in-memory
fixtures never open dev doors. Every failure labels its initial fixture origin:
`generated-fixture-trace` means an authored observation from a validated generated
initial state, not proof that app operations created that state. Author-supplied
fixtures retain `authored-fixture-trace`. Both fail acceptance on actual observed
violations; seed-only/synthetic table probes remain non-failing supplemental evidence.
Fixture-free tests report that no authored business cases ran. Finite passing
campaigns never prove security. Check bounds concrete exploration to 512 call/world
reservations and 1500 ms; exhaustion stays unproved and points to the full test.
`audit --json` retains all column audiences, grant writers, exclusions and uncovered
role/row/effect exposures. Audit returns the permission map with bounded check
evidence; run `test` for the full generated campaign. `audit --all` shows every
reason, without increasing the search. Declaring one effect does not cover the others.

## Reference

`invariant table.column read only P` protects a column value, derived values and value-dependent counts/existence/refusals. Mutation results, implicit final writes and explicit returned row objects are observers too. A query projection alone is not privacy: use column read rules to protect every operation. An authored projection excluding the column does not by itself disclose that value; filtering, ordering or copying it can. Audit JSON includes `column_audiences` (effective row rule plus private-owner gate). Unknown derived and opaque paths remain unproved.

`invariant table.read only P` protects read intent; insert, update and delete
name authority. `invariant table.column write only P` covers writes to a
column (insert, inserted insert-if-absent, update and both upsert arms).
A null creation or the declared `false` approval default is initialization, not an approval write. Mandatory creation presence needs whole-row insert/write or always intent; non-null/non-default creation still requires P. Tests judge each executed write in a committed mutation, including intermediate writes. `invariant table.column update only P` checks
only edits, retaining old-and-next-image semantics; use it when creation is permitted
to a different role (for example counter staff creating a ticket price). `invariant table always P` states row integrity.
An update policy over `.field` constrains **both old and next images**.
A policy saying status must not be override therefore also restricts editing
a row that was already override, even when changing it away from that status.
For the role authority of one named mutation, use operation-specific intent
(`invariant table.operation only P`) instead. An operator `provision` result excludes only its own executed roster write from authored read intent; later queries and other results remain checked. General next-image-only and
cross-operation transition assertions still need a separate reduction.
Predicates use the existing rule vocabulary: `.field`, `viewer`, `exists`,
comparisons, `and`, `or`, `not`. For a role write use the complete roster probe,
for example `invariant members.role write only exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']`
in a schema declaring those tables. A declaration never grants authority. Whole-row read intent also protects logical
existence: when occupancy totals are intentionally public but roster identities
are private, declare column read intent for those identities instead. Include
a service seat explicitly when the spec permits it; seats are ordinary checked
viewers, never implicitly exempt.

The implemented proof fragment exhaustively checks independent three-valued
rule predicates. A failed implication is unproved until an engine replay
establishes a counterexample. A schema-valid seeded snapshot is not a claim
that an application operation sequence reaches that state. Required-column
non-null assertions have a separate validation theorem. Read/count/sum/existence obligations are reported separately. A maintained aggregate with bare privacy keys can prove its contributor audience; a broader aggregate can yield a paired present/absent counterexample. Exact server `not before`/`expire at` bounds can prove a temporal read component independently of physical sweeps. General transitions and unsupported expressions remain unproved.
Read-through captures/live parents, complex derived aggregates, raw rule probes,
owned-field update bypasses, cascade/set-null and opaque jobs/workers need
separate reductions. An unresolved path blocks the whole declaration's proof.

Server delivery authorizes against the current server snapshot. A disconnected
device can retain an earlier snapshot until it receives a revocation; even
`until-revoked` cannot know a later server role. Synced current-role intent is
therefore unproved for device access. Retained history follows its leave policy.
These reports exclude physical timing and resource/meter side channels and
explicitly exclude the trusted system-principal boundary. Service seats remain
inside the ordinary-viewer check; there is no implicit seat exemption.

## Current roles and derived grants

A membership or assignment created while a user is an inspector can survive
their demotion. Conjoin the **current reader's role** with the assignment
branch, or revoke every derived grant transactionally whenever the role changes.
A grant writer's role does not establish the recipient's current authority.
With independent current-role intent, grant-lifetime advice points at this pattern; the argument-grant advice identifies
arguments written into reader-grant columns: validate the chosen principal's
role and relationship, even when the caller is privileged.
Global admin authority must come from a trusted operator or existing admin;
a group-creation `created` probe must remain scoped to that group everywhere.
`N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION` identifies role assignments trusted by later rules without independent delegation. Open enrollment may be intentional: compare each note with the specification. See `guide staging` for operator first-admin setup and `guide sharing` for parent authority.

## Required evidence and deadlines

If the spec requires a photo, use a required `image <=5000000` column or
`[image <=5000000] 1..4`; `?` permits omission and `[image] <=4` permits zero.
Uploads are verified media and asset reads inherit row and private-field rules.
Optional-evidence advice asks the builder to check intentional optionality.
Text `min..max` counts non-whitespace Unicode characters toward the minimum;
the maximum counts all characters. Stored text is not trimmed or rewritten.
A sweep changes state eventually: compare the deadline with server `now` in
the mutation too if writes must stop exactly at that instant.

## Existence observations

A lookup that says NOT_FOUND on absence followed by a denial saying FORBIDDEN
can reveal existence to callers admitted by a broader read rule. Use the same
code **and message** for hidden/absent/denied cases when existence is private,
or tighten the table read rule. Program `exists` is caller-filtered; probes
inside table rules inspect raw rows and can disclose predicates indirectly.
Inspect both paths in `audit`; a payload-only read check is insufficient.

## When an observer remains unproved

The report names the edge that lacks a reduction. Preserve the specification
while changing its enforcement; weakening an invariant to match a leaking
rule does not repair privacy.

- Maintained counts/sums: retain privacy fields as ordinary bare group keys
  and protect the aggregate with the same audience predicate. Recorded
  reference paths, calendar keys, derived values, private columns and temporal
  contributors need their own reduction; their names are not source fields.
- Owner fields: declare column-write intent too. `written by` authorizes
  patches even when the ordinary update rule denies. To prove a particular
  mutation's role authority, use a dominating current-role requirement and
  operation-specific intent over a role table the mutation cannot change.
  This does not prove every owner-field or old/next-image path.
- Raw rule probes: inspect the observing table and its writers. A protected
  target's read rule does not protect predicates probed from another rule.
  Apply the intended audience at that observer, or remove the raw dependency;
  the report retains the unsupported edge until it can establish implication.
- Cascade/set-null: automatic child changes bypass child write rules. An
  explicit authorized child mutation with `restrict` removes that automatic
  writer, but parent refusal/existence and transitive references remain
  separate observations. Read-through/live-parent authority requires a separate reduction.
- Jobs/native workers/actions: the audit names the trusted or opaque writer.
  An ordinary mutation with stable role requirements can prove that mutation's
  authority; arbitrary native/worker output cannot acquire a proof from a
  caller's role or a passing finite test.
- Schedules: use a stored `not before`/`expire at` lifetime for exact read
  boundaries, or compare server `now` in the write itself. A scheduled status
  update leaves the old status visible between deadline and sweep.

Default `test` challenges pure-schema applications through a private production
`serve`: unauthenticated calls, forged persona headers and development doors.
The host uses no pre-existing developer credentials or development mode. Applications needing
native/worker artifacts are explicitly not covered by that schema-only host.
Focused fixtures also enable short ordered mutation histories followed by
ordinary reads; traces record actual calls and current policy evaluation.
Named personas/service seats and enum argument combinations are enumerated
within reported limits. Asset challenges use real foreign-upload and verified
non-image ledgers; video arguments and unvisited histories remain named. Complete fixture stores preserve image/file ledgers, captures, read-through deadlines, maintained views and expiry. Histories include fixture principals and reference-row candidates, under a reported work cap. Neither
these finite campaigns nor an offline device's retained snapshot establishes
current-server revocation at a disconnected access.

### E_SECURITY_TARGET

E_SECURITY_TARGET means the invariant target does not name a declared table, column or effect. Correct the target name; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_SECURITY_INTENT

N_SECURITY_INTENT means access rules lack independent declared intent. Declare policy from the spec; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_BROAD_READ

N_BROAD_READ identifies universal or roster-wide readership; its message states the exact condition. Confirm the intended audience; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE

N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE means a stored grant may survive a later role change. Recheck the current reader role; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_ARGUMENT_GRANT

N_ARGUMENT_GRANT means an argument becomes a reader-grant column. Validate the recipient role and relationship; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE

N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE means optional media permits omitted evidence. Declare required media when the spec requires it; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_SWEEP_ONLY_DEADLINE

N_SWEEP_ONLY_DEADLINE means sweeps leave time between a deadline and cleanup. Enforce the deadline in the mutation; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE

N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE means absence and later denial can reveal different outcomes. Unify the code and message when existence is private; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

The isolated production HTTP campaign also signs in, refreshes the session,
logs out all sessions and retries both retired credentials against query,
mutation, schema, changes, sync, probe and refresh doors. It reports missing coverage
when ordinary identity or complete deployed worker artifacts are unavailable.
This checks server admission; a disconnected device can still retain old bytes.

### N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION

N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION identifies self-signup roles or role-grant operations that can confer authority trusted by other rules. Restrict assignment to authorized delegation; provision the first administrator with `guide staging`.

Identity rate refusals return HTTP `Retry-After` (seconds) and
`denied.retryAfterMs` (milliseconds), conservatively covering the 60-second
window. Wait that interval before retrying identity requests.

REL5 owns release transitions/old-client compatibility; MIG owns backfills and
staging clock control for day-two qualification.

### N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE

N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE identifies matching enforcement or a weaker copy made
from the same predicates. Its per-branch questions name admitted roles, row
relationships and lifecycle conditions. Read the original requirement and answer
those questions before editing: a simple confirmed policy may be correct.
The advisory comparison never adds proof assumptions or changes a verdict.

### N_ROLE_WIDE_READ

N_ROLE_WIDE_READ names a role-wide table branch or a grouped set of columns
with the same role audience. It asks whether the spec permits that audience on
other owners' rows and every value of the named state/category columns. Those
values are evidence to review, not an inferred lifecycle policy. Use a
board/projection table for shared fields, or narrow row/column rules to the
confirmed relationship and state conditions. Declare private-column intent separately.

`test` uses generation when fixtures are absent or contain no rows, including
an empty starter fixtures directory. Populated authored fixtures stay selected;
seeded read and integrity violations fail under that explicit starting-state
assumption. Generated payloads run authored creation mutations, preserving
requires, immutable copies and recipient-role checks; declined populations are
coverage gaps. A recorded role change or relationship transfer is shown in the
counterexample history; a stale starting snapshot is not such a history.

A completed `test` records concrete refutations under
`snapback/generated/security-test.json`. The next `check` reports matching
failures even when its short campaign cannot rediscover them. Exact source,
fixture bytes and CLI identity bind the record; edits invalidate it. No cached
passing result proves security. Every escalation note stays visible in short
output. Rule-changing deploy previews print exposure counts and demand a test
for the selected source/fixtures when a matching full finite campaign is absent.

### N_SELF_CLAIMED_RELATIONSHIP

N_SELF_CLAIMED_RELATIONSHIP means a caller can write their own principal into a relationship that another table's
raw read rule trusts. The note names the mutation, relationship column and
reader rule. Require an invitation or approval by the resource owner before
assigning that relationship; a caller's role alone does not establish parenthood
or membership. Confirm intentional open enrollment against the spec.

### N_CALLER_TIME

N_CALLER_TIME means a caller-supplied time reaches a field used by a deadline comparison with the
server clock. The note follows aliases and helper writes and names the field
and mutation. Use server `now` for the event time, or require an explicit
server-relative bound before the write when the spec allows historical input.
Staging clock and schedule diagnostics are owned by DX5; see `guide time`.


### N_INSERT_WRITE_INTENT

N_INSERT_WRITE_INTENT names creation paths that supply a column without an
edit operation's restriction. Confirm whether creation is deliberately broader;
otherwise guard insert and both upsert arms and declare column write intent.

### N_COLUMN_RESULT_EXPOSURE

N_COLUMN_RESULT_EXPOSURE names columns omitted by query projections or guarded
by column rules that another result/refusal may observe. Review that operation's
intended audience; this is advice, not proof of a leak.

Rule-changing deploy and deploy --check lead with outstanding FIX/REVIEW items
and the last test result, timestamp and age. Stale evidence is labeled explicitly.
Deploy --strict refuses reachable refutations; unproved intent and advice still
need review. A matching completed finite campaign is evidence, never proof.

### E_SECURITY_REVIEW

E_SECURITY_REVIEW means strict rule-changing deployment refused because reachable
security intent is refuted. Repair enforcement to match independent intent and
rerun test for the selected source and fixtures. Unproved notes and synthetic-only
observations do not cause this refusal.

### E_SESSION

E_SESSION means a supplied client session is malformed. Pass the complete Session
returned by guest, login, signup or your service, including principal, kind, token
and positive integer expiresAt milliseconds; the online token option accepts a bare
bearer. Constructors refuse before transport, storage, device or timer work.
Shape validation grants no authority; the server still decides session validity.

## Individual security review

Default check prints a verdict and the language-enforcement summary. Pending
gates always print; advisory decisions and notes require `check --review` (or
--verbose/JSON). A gate blocks; an advisory does not. The verdict counts pending
advisories and points to --review. A decision with a concrete campaign witness
shows the admitted operation, caller, arguments, old/new row and inferred
candidate. An upsert's existing-row arm is an **edit**. Opaque text, JSON and
asset values in these generated witnesses are redacted. A finite witness is
behavior to judge against the specification, never a proof of safety.
Questions about retained role grants and uploads with an explicit mandatory-media
contract require a repair or an individual disposition. They fail check without claiming an invariant refutation. Analyzer changes
invalidate older source-bound reviews; recheck current IDs and decisions.
Read the note, compare it with the specification, then use
`check --ack-security ID --reason 'specification rationale'` with its current ID
from check --review or `securityReviews[].id` in check --json. Repeat
`--ack-security ID` or pass comma-separated IDs to record several decisions
with the same reason atomically; an unknown/stale member writes none. Audience
family commands retain old member decisions when a new member appears; the new
member remains pending. Historical family commands select only their original,
still-current members. Bulk --ack-notes cannot clear these gates.
Commit snapback/security-reviews.json when the decision should travel with source.
Comments, line shifts and independent declarations preserve decisions. Changed
rule/column dependencies or analyzer revisions require review again; the record never grants authority or proves safety. Audit retains the full
notes and dispositions. JSON names each decision's exact-column/rule or conservative
declaration/writer binding scope. Advisories accept individual decisions and stay
non-failing; the grouped table questionnaire has no separate review ID.
Declared refutations still fail and cannot be acknowledged.
Deploy --strict retains its refutation gate; run check in acceptance before deploy.

Optional arguments and optional columns are valid, including deliberate loosening.
A required child media column validates an existing attachment; it does not require
a child row. Guarded optional attachments need no acknowledgment. When independent
parent integrity intent requires a child, require evidence before creating that
parent. Without such intent, a conditional child is an advisory spec question. Reasons use text 1..N when nonempty justification is required; optional
money and constant payment/approval flags need independent specification review.
Transition advice asks whether a generic setter can bypass a stricter decision or
whether eligibility checked at creation must be rechecked at approval. Different
duration windows on one column may be deliberate: confirm them against the spec.
Role-wide write questions ask whether a global role may write every resource.
Model the owner of this particular wall; see `guide rule-recipes --section "Wall ownership"`.

Test explores directed status/eligibility histories up to twelve mutations with a
separate finite successor bound. Check keeps its shared 512-work/1500ms limit.
Frontier, argument, signature and attempt cuts remain coverage gaps. Synthetic
observations are REVIEW, while actual admitted histories from validated generated
fixtures retain their generated-fixture-trace origin and can fail acceptance.

### N_RETAINED_ROLE_GRANT

N_RETAINED_ROLE_GRANT: A recipient role required when storing a grant is mutable and missing from a reader branch that trusts that grant, including column payload readers. Recheck current role or revoke derived grants. Intentional retained authority needs an individual dependency-bound disposition.

### N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE

N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE: An optional upload argument can be omitted in a direct nullable-media write or a skipped child that independent intent requires. When independent intent requires a child, either require the upload before parent creation or always create the child with deliberately optional media; skipping the child on a null upload violates that presence intent. Optional media columns and guarded optional attachments do not require acknowledgment; a required child column alone does not require a child row. Type-required media is already validated at each executed write. A direct non-null assertion also enforces media at that write; neither requires a skipped child row. Unknown helper returns and nullable expression alternatives remain advisory/test coverage, without an inferred argument-omission gate. A parent-omission gate needs a direct straight-line parent insert whose prefix does not inspect the upload argument; helper or conditional parent prefixes require independent integrity tests. Review an intentional exception with an individual dependency-bound disposition.

### N_CREATION_REASON

N_CREATION_REASON: Creating a row accepts a reason argument without a positive non-whitespace minimum. Compare the justification requirement with the spec; use text 1..N when required. This name-based question is advisory.

### N_CREATION_MONEY

N_CREATION_MONEY: Creating a row accepts omitted money. Model and require the amount when the spec requires a fee or payment; confirm intentional optional amounts against the spec. This name-based question is advisory.

### N_CONSTANT_APPROVAL

N_CONSTANT_APPROVAL: Creation stores a constant true approval/payment flag. A true constant does not verify payment or approval; record the required amount/evidence and the independently authorized decision. This name-based question is advisory.

### N_TRANSITION_BYPASS

N_TRANSITION_BYPASS: A generic enum setter reaches a state also reached by a stricter decision mutation. Apply the decision authority to every path reaching that state; write independent transition intent and run test.

### N_STALE_CREATION_CHECK

N_STALE_CREATION_CHECK: A creator checks a mutable eligibility column that a later literal-state transition does not recheck. One question groups the created table, dependency and decision sites; immutable ownership reads and non-state payload edits are excluded. Decide whether eligibility must remain current and write independent intent; test create, eligibility change, approval. Any literal enum destination distinct from its default creation state can raise this question, including filled; no approval-word list determines eligibility. True approval flags also qualify.

### N_DURATION_BOUNDS

N_DURATION_BOUNDS: Operations apply different server-relative duration bounds to one column. Compare the windows with the specification and make them consistent or document deliberate differences.

### N_ROLE_WIDE_WRITE

N_ROLE_WIDE_WRITE: A global role plus viewer attribution does not identify the owner of this resource. Ask whether any role member may write any row; model the target-to-owner relationship and guard it. See the Wall ownership rule recipe.

### E_SECURITY_NOTES

E_SECURITY_NOTES: the individual disposition file is malformed, cannot be written, or sources changed while recording a review. Inspect snapback/security-reviews.json and run check --json for current IDs. Supply a nonempty specification reason for one current note; neither bulk acknowledgments nor this file can clear an actual refutation.

### N_CREATE_EDIT_ASYMMETRY

N_CREATE_EDIT_ASYMMETRY: An argument-supplied column has a specific change guard on edit that creation does not imply. The question names that column, editor and guard, rather than a table-wide permission. Confirm creation authority against the specification; enforce the change guard at creation when required and test independent column write intent with non-null input. Exact changed-field comparisons in a stable single-write editor are supported. Target keys must be fixed inputs/IDs, and the editor cannot change its authority dependencies. Mixed conditions, changed branch aliases, mutable selectors, multiple target writes and early-return equivalents remain outside this heuristic, with generic insert inventory retained when applicable. Absence of advice is not a safety proof.

### N_PRIVATE_CREATE_EDIT_ASYMMETRY

N_PRIVATE_CREATE_EDIT_ASYMMETRY: A caller can initialize a private column without its change guard and outside its column read audience. This question ranks above ordinary asymmetry because the caller supplies private agency data it cannot read. Confirm who may supply this field at creation; enforce the guard when required and test independent column write intent with a non-null value. Read privacy alone does not define write authority; the question is advisory.

### N_EVIDENCE_REMOVAL

N_EVIDENCE_REMOVAL: Delete, detachment or explicit clearing can remove parent-linked media evidence. The question names removing operations and the parent reference, grouping the evidence columns. Required child fields alone do not require a surviving child; confirm last-child retention against the specification. Guard deletion or detachment transactionally, replace evidence or retire the parent, then declare independent parent integrity intent and run test. Ordinary non-null photo replacement is not removal; optional attachments without required creation linkage or independent integrity intent are excluded. This question is advisory.

### N_TABLE_QUESTIONNAIRE

N_TABLE_QUESTIONNAIRE: Role-wide access and restating invariants share one table questionnaire. Answer from the specification; JSON retains every underlying note and individual decision ID. The grouped line is advisory and has no separate ID. This is a specification question, never a proof or a new gate.

### N_APPROVAL_NOT_ENFORCED

N_APPROVAL_NOT_ENFORCED: An approval flag is written but not inspected by a lifecycle transition. Decide which transitions require approval, declare independent intent, and test both gating boolean values. This is a specification question, never a proof or a new gate.

### N_EDIT_AFTER_APPROVAL

N_EDIT_AFTER_APPROVAL: A checked value or media artifact can change without a positive creation-state restriction. Decide whether approval must be renewed after edits or replacements; declare column update intent. To freeze approved evidence use `immutable photo when .approved = true` from the complete rule recipe. This is a specification question, never a proof or a new gate.

### N_CHILD_EVIDENCE_PRESENCE

N_CHILD_EVIDENCE_PRESENCE: A separately created media child is not required by a parent transition. Required child fields do not require a child row. Declare parent integrity intent with the specification's draft-state exceptions. This is a specification question, never a proof or a new gate.

### N_TIME_TRANSITION

N_TIME_TRANSITION: A missed, overdue or expired state is written without a server-clock comparison. Confirm the relevant deadline against the specification and test both sides of its boundary. This is a specification question, never a proof or a new gate.

### N_GUARD_NOT_CONSUMED

N_GUARD_NOT_CONSUMED: A transition checks a related state or Boolean without consuming it. Decide whether the guard represents one-use authorization and test repeat transitions. This is a specification question, never a proof or a new gate.

### N_CALENDAR_DURATION

N_CALENDAR_DURATION: A 365-day multiple may differ from a calendar anniversary. Decide fixed elapsed days versus a calendar date and test leap-year boundaries. This is a specification question, never a proof or a new gate.

### N_DEPENDENT_WINDOW

N_DEPENDENT_WINDOW: A written deadline is bounded against now without consulting a dependent stored deadline. Confirm the window base and enforce the actual resource deadline when required. This is a specification question, never a proof or a new gate.

## Structural column protection

See `guide rule-recipes` for column protection and deploy refusals. Derived policies
accept individual decisions; authored refutations require repair. Concrete tests
retain unknown results and coverage gaps.

### N_DERIVED_WRITE_AUTHORITY

N_DERIVED_WRITE_AUTHORITY identifies an inferred candidate from authored change
guards or approval data. Enforce the candidate or record its individual specification
decision; only replayed violations refuse deploy, never a failed abstract implication.

A derived approval/edit violation requires approval to survive the edit. Clearing approval in that edit invalidates it; this is not a derived FIX. Authored `immutable c when P` still uses its stricter old-image constraint.

### N_PRESENCE_LOOSENING

N_PRESENCE_LOOSENING identifies a required-to-optional column change. Declare
presence intent or an individual specification decision. Successful check advances
a local generated baseline; production compares the selected release independently.
A fresh checkout has no invented predecessor. Deliberately optional evidence can
state `invariant evidence always .photo = null or .photo != null`.

exec
/bin/bash -lc "sed -n '1264,1290p' llp/3000.036.004-security-invariants-and-exposure.rfc.md; sed -n '1322,1338p' llp/3000.036.004-security-invariants-and-exposure.rfc.md; nl -ba llp/3000.036.004-security-invariants-and-exposure.rfc.md | sed -n '11,103p'; nl -ba snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/security/witnesses.rs | sed -n '106,163p'; sed -n '1,150p' snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/challenges/generated.rs" in /home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b28-dx11
 succeeded in 0ms:
Column update intent is spelled `invariant table.column update only P`; existing
`write` still includes insert and update. Multi-write observations may judge a
unique explicit target write when other stable writes touch different tables;
ambiguous repeated target writes and unstable policy dependencies stay unproved.
Histories interleave actors, argument alternatives, relationship changes and
successor operations. Media disclosure uses compiler-typed physical payload
witnesses; alternate-world media comparisons need a coupled verified ledger.
Role-wide branches gated by row values receive advisory N_ROLE_WIDE_READ when
the table carries private intent or media; suggest a projection/board table.
Escalation advice must constrain the assigned role by dominating argument guards
before naming it; an excluded target is not a witness.

`check` performs the sound static analysis and a bounded concrete campaign;
work exhaustion is explicit unproved coverage and points to `test`. A shared
short campaign deadline bounds exploration, including observer/world loops.
Already replayed violations remain failures; timeout never awards proof.
Memoizing exact implication input pairs is allowed; the cache is bounded,
process-local, and stores only the existing kernel's result. No assumptions
about fixture rows enter static proofs. `test` and `audit` retain the full
campaign bounds. Target cold/warm median check latency is at most five seconds
on the fifty wave-18 schemas.

Default check output is one short summary block with invariant counts and at
most five ranked actionable notes with source lines and concrete next steps.
Full reasons, individual unproved paths and complete note lists require
`--verbose` (JSON retains complete structured evidence). Refuted reachable
witnesses remain visible in compact form. Recommend `snapback4 test` when
authored business cases; generated-fixture only`. Explicit fixtures and saved
sources retain the business-case rule; `--fixture-only` keeps its meaning.

Check has a shared deterministic 512 call/world reservation budget, in addition
to a 1500 ms safety deadline. Each generated world, history replay step and
observer call reserves a unit (paired observers reserve their four calls).
Existing full-campaign caps remain upper bounds. Ordering is deterministic,
with initial authored typed read observers and column pairs before history
prefixes, then remaining synthetic worlds. The test-only operation matrix remains excluded
from check; `test` and `audit` use full campaign bounds. Exhaustion names `check
concrete exploration work budget exhausted; remaining paths unproved; run
snapback4 test for the full campaign` (or `time budget exhausted`). Counts and
complete uncovered details remain in JSON/verbose. No operation result or policy
judgment cache is added: only exact full RuleExpr implication input pairs are
memoized process-locally, bounded to 4096 entries.

The compact block always counts proved/refuted/unproved, labels supplemental

    34	aliases require every original member still to match; changed/deleted members
    35	refuse the whole batch. New members are not selected. The verdict counts still
    36	pending advisories and points to --review; their individual commands remain
    37	there. No new member changes a previously recorded member's acknowledgment.
    38	Batch expansion uses one captured sources/analyzer/note set and final source
    39	revalidation under the writer lock. One invalid ID writes no entries. Unknown IDs explain that
    40	they are stale, from a different source selection, or not decision IDs and
    41	point to `check --review --json`. Relevant semantic dependency changes still
    42	invalidate individual acknowledgments; unrelated declarations and comments do
    43	not. Version-1 records remain readable; witness-bearing decisions require a
    44	new global analyzer revision `sec11-1`, staling all older acknowledgments.
    45	The witness-associated codes in this increment are N_APPROVAL_NOT_ENFORCED,
    46	N_ARGUMENT_GRANT, N_INSERT_WRITE_INTENT and N_DERIVED_WRITE_AUTHORITY. Other
    47	questions explicitly retain their unsupported/no-witness status. The candidate
    48	is bound by its code, normalized diagnostic and semantic source dependency
    49	slice; operation/caller/arguments/images, generated ids, sample order and
    50	campaign timing are display/storage evidence only, never ID or binding inputs.
    51	
    52	A concrete campaign result associated with a question is shown as a witness:
    53	operation, caller, arguments, admitted effect, old/new image and candidate
    54	invariant. Questions may propose additional candidate invariants to exercise
    55	this output path: matching grant-column names to role literals, matching
    56	approval prefixes to boolean triggers, and reusing the edit restriction already
    57	printed by the write-advice detector. They are labeled inferred hypotheses and
    58	never alter the gate registry, authored invariants or check exit status.
    59	A decreasing upsert example replays two admitted writes on the engine from
    60	the observed generated fixture; it never fabricates old/new values.
    61	It remains evidence about an inferred specification question,
    62	never an authored contradiction or proof. Unsupported or exhausted campaigns
    63	say no witness was obtained, without inventing behavior. Acknowledgment
    64	records retain the displayed witness and candidate, alongside the rationale
    65	and semantic binding. Upsert wording explicitly says that its existing-row
    66	arm **edits**. Derived evidence cannot be used to override a reachable authored
    67	invariant refutation. The new witness path uses generated fixtures only, never application credential
    68	fixtures. Schema-typed text, bytes, JSON, asset values and unknown compound
    69	values are replaced by <redacted>; argument strings are redacted even when an
    70	enum. Retained scalar numerics/booleans, generated principals and row ids,
    71	source enum state names, operation names and candidate predicates describe
    72	the behavior. History records contain sanitized arguments and state only.
    73	A sentinel regression checks opaque redaction. Witness caches are generated
    74	presentation evidence and never decide authority or a verdict.
    75	
    76	The adjoining developer-loop changes preserve the contracts of LLP
    77	3000.000.000.001 and LLP 3000.015: refusal attribution and read-filter evidence
    78	are developer-owned diagnostics, never new disclosure in public refusal
    79	responses. Hooks expose `usePage.loading` and `usePage.why` as projections of
    80	`page.read`, retaining its completeness/freshness evidence. A first-run check
    81	hint makes the pinned React starter discoverable for projects that skipped
    82	init, without modifying their files. Staging reports name persisted clock
    83	offset; upload overload guidance names serialization/retry; proof docs say
    84	text must be on screen, and verification recipes use `serve-check`. Expo's
    85	starter offers a web file picker and native image picker.
    86	
    87	Qualification: targeted CLI/client regressions, witnesses for wave-28 011 F1,
    88	011 F2 and 019 F1, copies of all fifty schemas with baseline and SEC10 codes,
    89	packed consumer snippets/starters, affected selector and reference check.
    90	Darwin is unavailable in this Linux lane and is explicitly skipped. Finite
    91	campaign replay is not a security proof or a promise of builder preference.
    92	The CLI tests cover all JSON decision IDs, atomic mixed-invalid batches,
    93	comment/independent-edit stability, historical family additions, real grant
    94	attribution without public disclosure and authored-refutation precedence.
    95	React lifecycle tests exercise loading, denied and stale/partial page evidence;
    96	upload retry tests retain retry bounds and assert guidance. Packed tests run
    97	staging/probe scripts and typecheck/export Expo's picker. Clock tests check the
    98	printed retained offset. The 450-project new-gate census is in SEC10's lane:
    99	DX11 introduces no gate. --review retains the compact nondecision notes and
   100	individual questions (including the complete audience-family site list);
   101	JSON retains original diagnostics, never an invented questionnaire ID.
   102	Two independent reviews (Muse and fresh Codex) are retained under llp/reviews.
